исходного текста не удалены — это проверяет сам прибор и отказывается писать иначе.

## `insomnia-5ul3dkfi`

*Ряд 13 слов сбит до 2; сохранены 2002, 1997, Кристофер Нолан, Хиллари Зайц, американский психологический триллер, ремейк норвежского фильма того же названия.*

**Было:**

> The 2002 American psychological thriller directed by Christopher Nolan and written by Hillary Seitz is a remake of the 1997 Norwegian film of the same name. The story centers on two Los Angeles homicide detectives, Will Dormer (Al Pacino) and Hap Eckhart (Martin Donovan), sent to the small town of Nightmute, Alaska, to investigate the brutal murder of 17-year-old Kay Connell (Crystal Lowe). In the land of the midnight sun, where the sun never sets, Dormer suffers from insomnia, exacerbating his moral dilemmas. During a stakeout for the suspect, Dormer accidentally shoots and kills his partner Eckhart and, fearing the repercussions, decides to cover up his guilt by claiming the killer shot Eckhart. However, the real murderer, writer Walter Finch (Robin Williams), witnesses the incident and uses it to manipulate Dormer, proposing a mutual cover-up: Finch won't expose Dormer if he helps him evade charges for Kay's murder. The young local detective Ellie Burr (Hilary Swank), who admires Dormer's work, is assigned to investigate Eckhart's death. She gradually begins to suspect that not everything aligns with Dormer's account of events. The film delves into themes of guilt, moral compromise, and psychological pressure, intensified by the perpetual daylight that prevents Dormer from sleeping and thinking clearly. The soundtrack was composed by David Julyan, with cinematography by Wally Pfister. "Insomnia" received positive reviews from critics for Nolan's direction, its tense atmosphere, and strong performances, particularly from Pacino and Williams. The film grossed over $113 million worldwide against a budget of $46 million.​

**Стало:**

The story centers on two Los Angeles homicide detectives, Will Dormer (Al Pacino) and Hap Eckhart (Martin Donovan), sent to the small town of Nightmute, Alaska, to investigate the brutal murder of 17-year-old Kay Connell (Crystal Lowe). In the land of the midnight sun, where the sun never sets, Dormer suffers from insomnia, exacerbating his moral dilemmas. However, the real murderer, writer Walter Finch (Robin Williams), witnesses the incident and uses it to manipulate Dormer, proposing a mutual cover-up: Finch won't expose Dormer if he helps him evade charges for Kay's murder. The young local detective Ellie Burr (Hilary Swank), who admires Dormer's work, is assigned to investigate Eckhart's death. She gradually begins to suspect that not everything aligns with Dormer's account of events. The film delves into themes of guilt, moral compromise, and psychological pressure, intensified by the perpetual daylight that prevents Dormer from sleeping and thinking clearly. ## `glengarry-glen-ross-69s1ndrq`

*Ряд 13 слов сбит до 4; сохранены 1992, 1984, Джеймс Фоули, Дэвид Мэмет, Пулитцеровская премия, основа — его собственная пьеса. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 13 слов подряд («grossing 10 7 million in north america against a 12 5 millio…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> The 1992 American drama film directed by James Foley and written by David Mamet is based on his 1984 Pulitzer Prize-winning play. Set in Chicago, the story spans two days in the lives of four real estate salesmen: Ricky Roma (Al Pacino), Shelley "The Machine" Levene (Jack Lemmon), Dave Moss (Ed Harris), and George Aaronow (Alan Arkin). Their company announces a sales contest where only the top two salesmen will keep their jobs, leading to intense competition, manipulation, and even criminal acts among the employees. The film also features Kevin Spacey as office manager John Williamson and Alec Baldwin as motivational trainer Blake, a character created specifically for the film adaptation. Critically acclaimed for its powerful performances and intense script, the film was a box-office disappointment, grossing $10.7 million in North America against a $12.5 million budget. Nonetheless, it has since gained a cult following. Al Pacino received nominations for both the Academy Award and Golden Globe for Best Supporting Actor, while Jack Lemmon was awarded the Volpi Cup for Best Actor at the 49th Venice Film Festival. ​

**Стало:**

Set in Chicago, the story spans two days in the lives of four real estate salesmen: Ricky Roma (Al Pacino), Shelley "The Machine" Levene (Jack Lemmon), Dave Moss (Ed Harris), and George Aaronow (Alan Arkin). Their company announces a sales contest where only the top two salesmen will keep their jobs, leading to intense competition, manipulation, and even criminal acts among the employees. The film also features Kevin Spacey as office manager John Williamson and Alec Baldwin as motivational trainer Blake, a character created specifically for the film adaptation. Nonetheless, it has since gained a cult following. ## `perfect-days-6ihwn7fo`

*Ряд 13 слов сбит до 3; сохранены Япония и Германия как копродукция, Хираяма, Кодзи Якусё, уборщик общественных туалетов, Токио. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 10 слов подряд («2023 at the 76th cannes film festival where it competed…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> "Perfect Days" is a 2023 drama film directed by German filmmaker Wim Wenders, co-written with Japanese screenwriter Takuma Takasaki. A co-production between Japan and Germany, the film follows the life of Hirayama (Kōji Yakusho), a public toilet cleaner in Tokyo. Hirayama leads a structured and orderly life, diligently performing his duties with meticulous attention to detail. He finds joy in simple pleasures such as reading books, listening to music, and observing nature. However, his tranquil existence is disrupted when individuals from his past re-enter his life, prompting him to reassess his present and future. The film delves into themes of solitude, human connections, and the search for meaning in everyday life. "Perfect Days" premiered on May 25, 2023, at the 76th Cannes Film Festival, where it competed in the main competition and was awarded the Prize of the Ecumenical Jury, with Kōji Yakusho receiving the Best Actor Award.

**Стало:**

## `the-creature-walks-among-us-85ljj2xh`

*Ряд 13 слов сбит до 7 (остаток — само название трилогии); сохранены 1956, Джон Шервуд, научно-фантастический хоррор, третья и завершающая часть трилогии «Creature from the Black Lagoon».*

**Было:**

> The 1956 American science fiction horror film directed by John Sherwood is the third and final installment of the Creature from the Black Lagoon trilogy. The story follows the Gill-man, an amphibious humanoid creature who, after escaping from the Ocean Harbor Oceanarium in Florida, is recaptured by a team of scientists led by the ruthless Dr. William Barton (Jeff Morrow). During the capture, the creature suffers severe burns, leading the scientists to discover human-like skin beneath its scales and dormant lungs. They perform surgery to transform the Gill-man into a land-dwelling being capable of breathing air and attempt to integrate him into human society. However, Barton's paranoia and jealousy over his wife Marcia (Leigh Snowden) drive him to murder their guide Jed Grant (Gregg Palmer) and frame the creature. Realizing the injustice, the Gill-man breaks free, kills Barton, and returns to the ocean, despite no longer being able to breathe underwater. The film concludes Universal's original classic monster series and presents a darker, more psychologically complex narrative than its predecessors. The creature was portrayed by Don Megowan on land and Ricou Browning underwater.

**Стало:**

## `lightyear-4aoj6de6`

> A 2022 American animated science fiction action-adventure film produced by Pixar Animation Studios and released by Walt Disney Pictures. Directed by Angus MacLane, known for his work on "Finding Dory" and "Toy Story" films, the movie serves as a spin-off of the "Toy Story" series, focusing on the origin story of Buzz Lightyear, who was portrayed as a toy in the original series but is depicted here as a real space ranger. The plot follows Buzz Lightyear (voiced by Chris Evans), a courageous space ranger who, along with his crew, becomes stranded on a hostile planet after a failed mission. Determined to return home, Buzz and his companions face numerous challenges, including battles with robots and overcoming personal doubts. Buzz's team includes his commander and best friend Alisha Hawthorne (Uzo Aduba), her granddaughter Izzy Hawthorne (Keke Palmer), clumsy recruit Morrison (Taika Waititi), and seasoned but cynical Darby Steel (Dale Soules). A notable character is a robotic cat named SOX (Peter Sohn), providing comic relief and technical support. The film explores themes of friendship, responsibility, and courage, showcasing Buzz's journey in learning to work as a team and accept help from others. The visual effects and animation have been praised for their detail and realism, while the musical score by Michael Giacchino enhances the film's emotional impact.

## `the-bad-news-bears-8ffoao97`

*Ряд 14 слов сбит до 3: сохранены 1976, Michael Ritchie, Bill Lancaster, Morris Buttermaker, Walter Matthau, бывший игрок низшей лиги, чистильщик бассейнов, алкоголик.*

**Было:**

> The 1976 American sports comedy film directed by Michael Ritchie and written by Bill Lancaster follows Morris Buttermaker (Walter Matthau), a former minor league baseball player turned pool cleaner and alcoholic. At the request of lawyer Bob Whitewood (Ben Piazza), Buttermaker agrees to coach the Bears, a team of misfit children rejected by other Little League teams in Southern California. Initially, the team suffers humiliating defeats, but the dynamics change when Buttermaker recruits his former student and stepdaughter Amanda Whurlitzer (Tatum O'Neal), a talented and confident pitcher, along with local troublemaker and skilled athlete Kelly Leak (Jackie Earle Haley). With their addition, the team starts to win games, but Buttermaker faces a moral quandary as he realizes that an overemphasis on winning could harm the children's well-being. In the championship game against the Yankees, whose coach Roy Turner (Vic Morrow) exhibits aggressive behavior, even striking his own son, Buttermaker chooses to play all his team members, including the weakest, emphasizing participation and team spirit over victory. The film was critically acclaimed for its honest and witty portrayal of youth sports, earning a BAFTA nomination for Best Actor (Walter Matthau), and the screenplay by Bill Lancaster received the Writers Guild of America Award.

**Стало:**

> The 2024 Italian teen romance film, directed by Alessandro Genovesi, is based on Erin Doom's novel of the same name. The story follows eight-year-old Nica, who becomes orphaned after her parents die in a car accident and is sent to the oppressive Sunnycreek Orphanage, where she befriends a girl named Adeline. Years later, as a teenager, Nica is adopted by Anna and Norman Milligan along with Rigel, a moody teenager favored by the headmistress, Margaret. In their new home, Rigel is hostile toward Nica. Flashbacks reveal that at Sunnycreek, all children except Rigel were subjected to physical and emotional abuse by Margaret. At school, Nica receives a black rose, symbolizing tormented and obsessive love, but Rigel denies involvement. Classmate Lionel asks Nica out, leading to a fight between him and Rigel. Gradually, romantic feelings develop between Nica and Rigel, but their relationship is complicated by past traumas and internal conflicts. The film was released on Netflix on April 4, 2024.

**Было:**

> The 1990 American drama film directed by David F. Wheeler is an adaptation of the eponymous play by British playwright Heathcote Williams. The film follows two British friends, Graham (Al Pacino) and Ray (Paul Guilfoyle), who spend their time wandering through London discussing greyhound racing. Their lives are marked by cynicism and cruelty, deriving pleasure from psychological games and acts of violence against random victims. One day, they encounter a famous actor, David (Joseph Maher), whom they decide to humiliate and assault, expressing their disdain for societal values. The film delves into themes of alienation, senseless violence, and the destructive consequences of idleness. Notably, Al Pacino, a fan of the original play, not only took on the lead role but also produced the project. The film was shot in the late 1980s and had its premiere at the Museum of Modern Art in New York City in March 1990 but did not receive a theatrical release. In 2007, it was released on DVD as part of The Al Pacino Box Set. Despite the involvement of renowned actors, the film did not achieve critical success and remains relatively obscure to the general audience. ​

## `next-goal-wins-b2d5ftqw`

*Ряд 14 слов сбит до 5: сохранены Toronto International Film Festival, 10 сентября 2023, 17 ноября 2023. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 12 слов подряд («the first openly transgender athlete to compete in a world c…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> The 2023 American sports comedy-drama film directed by Taika Waititi is based on the 2014 documentary of the same name and depicts the true story of the American Samoa national football team. In 2001, the team suffered a devastating 0–31 loss to Australia, marking the worst defeat in international football history. A decade later, following another humiliating performance at the Pacific Games, the nation's football federation decides to hire a new coach. Thomas Rongen (Michael Fassbender), a Dutch-American coach recently dismissed from the U.S. Soccer Federation, reluctantly accepts the offer. Upon arrival, Rongen faces challenges including limited resources, unskilled players, and cultural differences. His relationship with Jaiyah Saelua (Kaimana), a transgender defender and the first openly transgender athlete to compete in a World Cup qualifier, is initially strained. As Rongen confronts personal demons, including guilt over his daughter's death, he adapts his coaching methods, builds trust with the team, and inspires them to strive for success. The film culminates in a match against Tonga, where the team secures a long-awaited 2–1 victory. The narrative explores themes of perseverance, acceptance, cultural identity, and the transformative power of sports. The cast also includes Oscar Kightley, David Fane, Rachel House, Beulah Koale, Will Arnett, and Elisabeth Moss. The film premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival on September 10, 2023, and was released theatrically on November 17, 2023.​

**Стало:**

## `tom-and-jerry-the-fast-and-the-furry-yiyglgut`

*Ряд был 14 слов, стал 3. Сохранены все имена (Grammy, Squirty, Gorthan, Steed Dirkly, Dr.), кавычки "Soccer Mom", число четверых детей; хвост стыкуется с "Dr. Professor".*

**Было:**

> The 2005 American animated action-adventure comedy film, directed by Bill Kopp, is the third direct-to-video feature starring Tom and Jerry, released in celebration of the duo's 65th anniversary since their debut in 1940. The story begins with Tom (voiced by Bill Kopp) and Jerry destroying their home during one of their classic chases, leading to their eviction by their owner. In search of new accommodation, they learn about a reality race show called the "Fabulous Super Race," where the winner is awarded a luxurious mansion. Determined to win, Tom and Jerry head to Globwobbler Studios in Hollywood, California, to enter the race. Other contestants include elderly Grammy with her dog Squirty, dark lord and florist Gorthan, superstar Steed Dirkly, single mother of four known as "Soccer Mom," and a scientist named Dr. Professor, who accidentally eliminates himself before the race due to a mishap with antimatter. The race kicks off in Hollywood and spans various locations such as Mexico, the Amazon jungle, Antarctica, Australia, and Borneo. Throughout the competition, participants face numerous challenges and modify their vehicles for travel across land, sea, and air. One by one, the racers are eliminated, leaving Tom and Jerry as the final contenders. In the last leg, they are required to traverse the globe in just five minutes using jet planes. Both cross the finish line simultaneously, resulting in a tie. The race organizer, Jacques "J.W." Wren, declares that a tiebreaker race must be held, as per the contract. Unwilling to continue, Tom and Jerry attack Wren and seize the keys to the mansion, deciding to share the prize. The film premiered in select U.S. theaters on September 3, 2005, followed by releases on VHS and DVD on October 11, 2005, and later on Blu-ray on April 5, 2011. It received mixed reviews from critics; while some praised its fast-paced plot and humor, others criticized it for predictability and repetitive gags.​

**Стало:**

However, his actions draw the attention of the Ministry of Information, particularly his childhood friend Jack Lint (Michael Palin), now working in the interrogation department. Soon, Sam becomes a target of the system, accused of subversive activities. The film also features Robert De Niro as Harry Tuttle, a renegade engineer combating the bureaucracy, and Bob Hoskins as Spoor, a maintenance officer. "Brazil" delves into themes of totalitarianism, excessive bureaucracy, individual freedom, and the conflict between reality and fantasy. The film received critical acclaim for its visual style, complex narrative, and satirical portrayal of society but achieved moderate commercial success, grossing approximately $9.9 million in the US against a $15 million budget. Over time, "Brazil" has become a cult classic, lauded for its prescient commentary on societal issues and unique artistic vision.

> The 1989 American sports fantasy drama, directed by Phil Alden Robinson and based on Canadian novelist W. P. Kinsella's book "Shoeless Joe," follows Iowa farmer Ray Kinsella (Kevin Costner) who hears a mysterious voice whispering, "If you build it, he will come." Interpreting this as a directive, Ray constructs a baseball field in his cornfield, risking financial ruin and facing skepticism from those around him. Upon completion, the field becomes a gathering place for the ghosts of baseball legends, including Shoeless Joe Jackson (Ray Liotta) and other members of the 1919 Chicago White Sox team banned from the sport. Seeking understanding, Ray embarks on a journey that leads him to reclusive author Terence Mann (James Earl Jones) and former player Archie "Doc" Graham (Burt Lancaster). The film delves into themes of faith, redemption, familial bonds, and the pursuit of dreams, emphasizing the significance of heeding one's inner voice. Acclaimed by critics, "Field of Dreams" received three Academy Award nominations, including Best Picture, and has become a beloved classic among baseball enthusiasts and film audiences alike.

> An American legal thriller released in 2019, directed by Todd Haynes and written by Mario Correa and Matthew Michael Carnahan. The film is based on the true story of attorney Robert Bilott (portrayed by Mark Ruffalo), who embarks on a prolonged legal battle against the chemical giant DuPont, accusing them of environmental contamination with toxic substances. Formerly a defender of corporate interests, Bilott takes on the case of farmer Wilbur Tennant (Bill Camp), whose cattle suffered mass deaths due to poisoned water. Through his investigation, Bilott uncovers that DuPont had been concealing information for decades about the dangers of perfluorooctanoic acid (PFOA), a chemical used in the production of Teflon, and its detrimental effects on human and animal health. Facing immense opposition, including threats to his career and personal life, Bilott persists in his quest for justice, risking everything to protect public welfare. The film addresses pressing issues of corporate accountability, environmental safety, and the power of an individual standing against a formidable system.

> The 1995 American romantic comedy-drama directed by Sydney Pollack is a remake of Billy Wilder's 1954 film of the same name, which was based on the 1953 play "Sabrina Fair" by Samuel A. Taylor. The story centers on Sabrina Fairchild (Julia Ormond), the daughter of the chauffeur for the wealthy Larrabee family in Long Island. She has been infatuated with the family's younger son, charming playboy David Larrabee (Greg Kinnear), since childhood, though he has never noticed her. After an internship at Vogue in Paris, Sabrina returns home transformed into a sophisticated and elegant woman, catching David's attention despite his engagement to Elizabeth Tyson (Lauren Holly), the daughter of a powerful businessman. David's older brother, workaholic Linus Larrabee (Harrison Ford), becomes concerned that David's involvement with Sabrina could jeopardize a significant business merger. To prevent this, Linus intervenes to distract Sabrina, but unexpectedly finds himself falling in love with her. The film explores themes of love, class differences, and personal growth, blending romantic elements with drama and comedy. The cast also includes Nancy Marchand, John Wood, Richard Crenna, Angie Dickinson, Dana Ivey, and Fanny Ardant. The musical score was composed by John Williams, and the song "Moonlight," performed by Sting, received an Academy Award nomination. Despite positive critical reception, the film achieved moderate box office success, grossing approximately $87 million against a budget of $50–58 million.

> An American action-adventure television series that aired in syndication from 1958 to 1961, created by Ivan Tors. The main character, Mike Nelson (Lloyd Bridges), is a former U.S. Navy diver and member of the Coast Guard who works as a freelance diver, undertaking dangerous missions such as rescuing people, recovering sunken objects, and combating criminals. Storylines often involve rescuing pilots, children, sunken ships, and even nuclear warheads. Filming took place in various locations, including California, Florida, and the Bahamas. Underwater scenes were shot using studio tanks and on-location sites. The series became one of the most popular in the U.S., attracting up to 40 million viewers weekly. It also featured future stars like Leonard Nimoy, Jack Nicholson, and Bridges' sons Beau and Jeff. At the end of each episode, Bridges addressed the audience with environmental messages and safety tips. The show significantly influenced the popularity of scuba diving and inspired many to take up the activity.

## `t-a-t-u-ifea2ew1`

*Ряд 17 → 3 слова; сохранены оба альбома с годами (2001, 2002), обе песни, платина в Европе и первенство иностранной группы в Японии.*

**Было:**

> Russian pop duo formed in Moscow in 1999 by producer Ivan Shapovalov and composer Alexander Voitinsky. The group consisted of Lena Katina and Yulia Volkova, former members of the children's ensemble Neposedy. They gained notoriety for their provocative image and lesbian-themed content, which sparked widespread public attention. Their debut album, "200 Po Vstrechnoy" (2001), achieved commercial success in Eastern Europe, and its English-language counterpart, "200 km/h in the Wrong Lane" (2002), brought international fame with hits like "All the Things She Said" and "Not Gonna Get Us." The album was certified platinum in Europe and became the first album by a foreign group to reach number one in Japan. In 2003, the duo represented Russia at the Eurovision Song Contest with the song "Ne ver', ne boysia," securing third place. Subsequent albums, including "Dangerous and Moving" (2005) and "Vesyolye Ulybki" (2008), solidified their global presence. The group officially disbanded in 2011, but the members occasionally reunited for special performances, such as the opening ceremony of the 2014 Sochi Olympics. t.A.T.u. remains one of the few Russian music acts to achieve significant international success, with over 8 million records sold worldwide.

**Стало:**

> An American anthology television series that PBS carried across 1982 through 1993. The show featured a collection of standalone dramatic presentations, adapting works by renowned American authors and playwrights. It opened on January 12, 1982 with the episode "The Shady Hill Kidnapping," written and narrated by John Cheever and directed by Paul Bogart. Over 13 seasons, "American Playhouse" offered viewers a diverse array of stories, ranging from classic literary adaptations to contemporary dramas exploring complex themes and human relationships. The series served as a launching pad for many actors, including John Malkovich, Laura Linney, and Ben Stiller, providing them with opportunities to showcase their talents in various roles. Additionally, "American Playhouse" was among the first U.S. television programs to implement audio description services for visually impaired audiences, highlighting its commitment to accessibility and inclusivity. The final episode, "In the Wings: Angels in America on Broadway," aired on January 1, 1994, offering viewers a behind-the-scenes look at the creation of Tony Kushner's acclaimed play. Throughout its run, the series received critical acclaim and made a significant contribution to bringing American dramatic literature to television audiences.

> Avowed is a single-player role-playing game with first-person and third-person perspectives, developed by Obsidian Entertainment and published by Xbox Game Studios. Released on February 18, 2025, for Windows and Xbox Series X/S, the game is set in the world of Eora, known from the Pillars of Eternity series, specifically in the region of the Living Lands. Players take on the role of an envoy from the Aedyr Empire, tasked with investigating a mysterious plague called the "Dream Scourge." Gameplay combines the use of magic, melee weapons, and firearms, allowing players to mix spells and physical attacks for powerful combinations. The game features several large zones to explore and a dialogue system where player choices influence the storyline and relationships with companions. Development began in 2018, and after several reboots, the project was completed under the direction of Carrie Patel. Avowed received positive reviews from critics for its deep narrative, well-developed characters, and engaging combat system.

> The 1993 French-American surrealist comedy-drama directed by Emir Kusturica follows Axel Blackmar (Johnny Depp), a young New Yorker working as a fish tagger who lives in a world of dreams. In Arizona, Axel meets the eccentric widow Elaine Stalker (Faye Dunaway), who dreams of flying, and her adopted daughter Grace (Lili Taylor), who is prone to depression and obsessed with suicide. Axel becomes entangled in complex relationships with both women, leading to emotional and psychological conflicts. The film is filled with surreal imagery, including flying fish and scenes where reality intertwines with imagination. The soundtrack is composed by Goran Bregović and Iggy Pop, featuring the song "In the Death Car". "Arizona Dream" won the Silver Bear – Special Jury Prize at the 43rd Berlin International Film Festival and has become a cult classic known for its unique atmosphere and profound themes about the search for meaning and dreams.​

## `gotham-city-impostors-p4crtws0`

**Было:**

> A multiplayer first-person shooter developed by Monolith Productions and published by Warner Bros. Interactive Entertainment, set in the DC Comics universe. Unlike most Batman-related games, this one does not feature the Dark Knight himself or his iconic villains. Instead, players take control of impostors inspired by either Batman or the Joker, engaging in chaotic battles across Gotham's streets. The game offers fast-paced action with extensive character customization, allowing players to equip various weapons, gadgets, and abilities such as rocket launchers, grappling hooks, and spring-loaded boots. Gotham City Impostors stands out with its humorous and over-the-top art style, adding a unique twist to the DC universe. While praised for its quirky style, fun gameplay, and customization depth, the game suffered from online issues, including technical problems and a declining player base. Initially released as a paid game for PC, PlayStation 3, and Xbox 360, it later transitioned to a free-to-play model, but its online servers were shut down in 2021.

> The 2024 Canadian crime drama television series, created by Tassie Cameron, René Balcer, and Dick Wolf, is an adaptation of the American series "Law & Order: Criminal Intent" and is set in Toronto, Ontario. The storyline focuses on detectives from the Specialized Criminal Investigations Unit of the fictional Toronto Police Department, who investigate high-profile homicides and corruption cases inspired by real events. The main characters are Detective Sergeant Henry Graff (Aden Young) and Detective Sergeant Frankie Bateman (Kathleen Munroe), working under the supervision of Inspector Vivienne Holness (Karen Robinson). They are assisted by Deputy Crown Attorney Theo Forrester (K.C. Collins), forensic pathologist Dr. Lucy Da Silva (Nicola Correia-Damude), and tech expert Mark Yohannes (Araya Mengesha). The first season, comprising 10 episodes, premiered on Citytv on February 22, 2024, and became the top-rated prime-time drama of the year in Canada, attracting 1.1 million viewers for its debut. The series was renewed for a second and third season in June 2024. In the second season, which began in 2025, the detectives continue to tackle complex cases involving politics, business, and organized crime, employing a psychological approach to crime-solving and highlighting the moral dilemmas and personal motivations of the perpetrators.

**Было:**

> The 2007 documentary film directed by Michael Moore, initially presented under the title "Captain Mike Across America" at the Toronto International Film Festival on September 7, 2007. The film documents Moore's 62-city tour across the United States in 2004, aiming to increase young voter turnout during the presidential election between George W. Bush and John Kerry. Moore visits college campuses, organizing rallies and events featuring celebrities such as Eddie Vedder (Pearl Jam), Roseanne Barr, Joan Baez, Tom Morello (Rage Against the Machine), R.E.M., Steve Earle, and Viggo Mortensen. The film showcases the enthusiasm and activism of youth striving to influence the political process. Following its premiere, Moore re-edited the film and released it under the title "Slacker Uprising" on September 23, 2008, making it available for free download in the U.S. and Canada, marking it as one of the first feature-length films legally distributed in this manner. The film received mixed reviews from critics; some viewed it as a nostalgic and self-promotional project, while others highlighted its significance in the context of youth political engagement.​

**Стало:**

> The 2010 Canadian comedy-drama directed by Richard J. Lewis, based on the 1997 novel by Mordecai Richler and scripted by Michael Konyves, chronicles the life of Barney Panofsky (Paul Giamatti), a television producer reflecting on his tumultuous past. Spanning four decades and two countries, the narrative begins with Barney's bohemian youth in Rome, where he marries the eccentric artist Clara (Rachelle Lefevre), whose tragic death leaves a lasting impact. His second marriage to an unnamed woman (Minnie Driver), a member of the upper class, proves unsuccessful. During their wedding reception, Barney meets Miriam (Rosamund Pike), whom he believes to be his true love. Their relationship blossoms despite obstacles, and Miriam becomes his third wife and the mother of his children. Barney's father, Izzy Panofsky (Dustin Hoffman), a former police officer, plays a significant role in his life, offering guidance and support. The film also delves into the mysterious disappearance of Barney's best friend, Boogie (Scott Speedman), leading to suspicions of murder. As the story unfolds, Barney grapples with accusations, personal demons, and declining health, including memory loss. Despite his flaws, he remains devoted to Miriam, who continues to visit him in a nursing home. The film received critical acclaim, with Paul Giamatti winning the Golden Globe Award for Best Actor in a Motion Picture – Musical or Comedy.

## `return-to-sender-qikwkys5`

**Было:**

> The 2015 American psychological thriller directed by Fouad Mikati, written by Patricia Beauchamp and Joe Gossett, follows nurse Miranda Wells (Rosamund Pike) living in a small town, aspiring to become a surgical nurse. Her friend sets her up on a blind date with a man named Kevin, but on the day of the date, a different man arrives, whom she assumes is Kevin. He turns out to be William Finn (Shiloh Fernandez), who assaults and rapes her. The real Kevin arrives later, finds Miranda injured, and calls the police, leading to William's arrest. Following the attack, Miranda faces challenges: her house becomes unsellable, and a tremor in her hand prevents her from pursuing her surgical career. She begins writing letters to William in prison; despite initial resistance, he responds, and they develop a peculiar relationship, with Miranda visiting him regularly and engaging in flirtatious conversations. Upon his release, William visits her home, where she serves him lemonade laced with antifreeze, then restrains him in her basement. She confesses to poisoning him, as well as her father's dog, and allowing her mother to die. The film concludes with Miranda informing her father that William will not be coming around anymore.

> A 2000 role-playing video game developed by BioWare and published by Interplay Entertainment, Baldur’s Gate II: Shadows of Amn is the sequel to Baldur’s Gate and is based on the Advanced Dungeons & Dragons 2nd Edition rules. The player assumes the role of the Child of Bhaal, a descendant of the god of murder, who is kidnapped by the mage Irenicus and subjected to mysterious experiments. After escaping captivity, the protagonist finds themselves in the city of Athkatla, the heart of Amn, filled with political intrigue, criminal factions, and magical dangers. Throughout the journey, players must assemble a party of allies, navigate complex moral choices, explore dangerous dungeons, and ultimately decide their fate as a Bhaalspawn. The game is praised for its deep role-playing mechanics, branching dialogues, multiple paths to completion, and its real-time combat system with tactical pause. Baldur’s Gate II: Shadows of Amn is widely regarded as one of the greatest RPGs of all time, earning critical acclaim for its rich world-building, narrative depth, and strategic gameplay.

*Ряд 14 слов срезан до 5 (остаток — само имя фестиваля); сохранены три актёра, 24 мая 2024, 77-й Каннский кинофестиваль, номинация на «Золотую пальмовую ветвь» и приз жюри; запрещённое «premiered on» убрано. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 12 слов подряд («premiere rasoulof was sentenced to eight years in prison by …») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> A 2024 Iranian-German-French political drama directed by Mohammad Rasoulof. The plot centers on Iman, an investigative judge in Tehran's Revolutionary Court, who grapples with paranoia amid escalating political protests following the death of a young woman. When his gun mysteriously disappears, Iman begins to distrust his wife and daughters. The film combines fictional narrative with real footage of the 2022–2023 protests in Iran, which were violently suppressed by authorities. Starring Soheila Golestani, Missagh Zareh, and Mahsa Rostami, it premiered on May 24, 2024, at the 77th Cannes Film Festival, where it was nominated for the Palme d'Or and won the Special Jury Prize. Prior to the premiere, Rasoulof was sentenced to eight years in prison by Iranian authorities but managed to flee to Germany and attended the Cannes red carpet with the cast. The film received critical acclaim and was released in France on September 18, 2024, grossing $6.2 million.

> An American teen sitcom that Fox carried for eight years, from August 23, 1998 until May 18, 2006. The series follows the lives of a group of six teenagers living in the fictional town of Point Place, Wisconsin, from 1976 to 1979. The main characters include Eric Forman (Topher Grace), a witty and sarcastic young man living with his parents, Red (Kurtwood Smith) and Kitty (Debra Jo Rupp); Donna Pinciotti (Laura Prepon), a feminist and Eric's neighbor, with whom he shares a romantic relationship; Steven Hyde (Danny Masterson), a rebellious friend of Eric who is later adopted by the Forman family; Michael Kelso (Ashton Kutcher), a handsome but dim-witted guy; Jackie Burkhart (Mila Kunis), a spoiled and wealthy girl who initially dates Kelso and later Hyde; and Fez (Wilmer Valderrama), a foreign exchange student with a mysterious background. The series explores themes of teenage love, friendship, family dynamics, and social issues of the 1970s, including sexual attitudes, drug use, and distrust of the government. The show also features supporting characters such as Bob Pinciotti (Don Stark), Donna's father, and Laurie Forman (Lisa Robin Kelly), Eric's older sister. The series was praised for its humor and accurate portrayal of 1970s culture, becoming a cult favorite among audiences.

## `the-promise-vzesn4yr`

*Ряд 14 слов срезан до 2; сохранены смешанные отзывы критиков, провал в прокате, около $12 млн сборов и бюджет $90 млн. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 12 слов подряд («a 2016 american epic historical war drama film directed by t…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> A 2016 American epic historical war drama film directed by Terry George, who co-wrote the screenplay with Robin Swicord. Set during the final years of the Ottoman Empire, the film explores a love triangle between Armenian medical student Mikael Boghosian (Oscar Isaac), American journalist Chris Myers (Christian Bale), and Ana Khesarian (Charlotte Le Bon), an Armenian woman raised in France. Mikael leaves his fiancée Maral (Angela Sarafyan) in his hometown of Siroun to pursue medical studies in Constantinople. There, he meets Ana, who is in a relationship with Chris, leading to a mutual attraction between Mikael and Ana. As the Armenian genocide unfolds, Mikael is conscripted into a labor camp but manages to escape. Returning to Siroun, he finds his village devastated and many residents killed. Together with Ana and a group of orphans, they join the resistance on Mount Musa Dagh, where Armenians fend off attacks from the Ottoman army. Eventually, the French navy evacuates the survivors, but Ana drowns during the bombardment. Mikael adopts his niece Yeva and emigrates with her to Watertown, Massachusetts. The film received mixed reviews from critics and was a box office bomb, grossing approximately $12 million against a $90 million budget. However, the filmmakers emphasized that the primary goal was to raise awareness about the Armenian genocide rather than achieve commercial success. The soundtrack features "The Promise" by Chris Cornell, which became his final single before his death.

**Стало:**

> The 2024 British animated comedy film, produced by Aardman Animations in association with the BBC and Netflix, is the sixth installment in the Wallace & Gromit series and the second feature-length film following "The Curse of the Were-Rabbit" (2005). This story marks the return of the villainous penguin Feathers McGraw from "The Wrong Trousers" (1993), who seeks revenge on Wallace and Gromit by turning Wallace's latest invention against them. The film premiered on October 27, 2024, at the American Film Institute, followed by a broadcast on BBC One and BBC iPlayer in the United Kingdom on December 25, and became available on Netflix internationally on January 3, 2025. ## `baldur-s-gate-y8xzro8n`

*Ряд 14 → 4 слова; сохранены 1998, BioWare, Interplay Entertainment, Baldur’s Gate, Infinity Engine и оба утверждения о влиянии на жанр.*

**Было:**

> A 1998 role-playing video game developed by BioWare and published by Interplay Entertainment, Baldur’s Gate became one of the most influential RPGs of its time, revitalizing interest in the genre and setting a foundation for future Infinity Engine games. Based on the Advanced Dungeons & Dragons 2nd Edition rules, the game is set in the Forgotten Realms. The player assumes the role of an orphan raised by the wise mage Gorion, whose sudden death at the hands of a mysterious assassin sets the protagonist on a perilous journey along the Sword Coast. As the adventure unfolds, players uncover political intrigue, an iron crisis, magical conspiracies, and the secrets of their own heritage. The game allows for a highly customizable party, featuring a diverse cast of companions with distinct personalities, backstories, and relationship dynamics. Baldur’s Gate is known for its open-ended structure, deep role-playing mechanics, branching dialogues, and strategic real-time combat with a pause system. It received critical acclaim for its immersive world, engaging story, and player-driven choices, becoming a cornerstone of modern RPGs.

**Стало:**

> A 2002 American crime comedy directed by Harold Ramis, serving as a sequel to the 1999 film "Analyze This." The film stars Robert De Niro as mobster Paul Vitti and Billy Crystal as psychiatrist Dr. Ben Sobel. The story follows Vitti, who, nearing the end of his sentence in Sing Sing prison, faces threats from fellow inmates and guards. To secure his release, he feigns mental instability by singing songs from "West Side Story," catching the attention of his former therapist, Ben Sobel. The FBI enlists Ben to assess Vitti's mental state, and after evaluation, Vitti is released into Ben's custody for further therapy. Vitti moves into Ben's home and attempts to lead a legitimate life, taking on various jobs, including consulting on a mafia-themed TV show. However, his past continues to haunt him as he confronts threats from the Rigazzi crime family and suspicion from the FBI, which believes he is orchestrating a new crime. Vitti and Ben become entangled in a complex situation involving the heist of a gold transport, culminating in Vitti aiding in exposing the Rigazzi family and severing ties with his criminal past. The film received mixed reviews from critics and grossed $55 million worldwide against a $60 million budget.

## `collateral-beauty-eovu8irb`

*Ряд 14 → 3 слова; сохранены 2016, David Frankel, Allan Loeb, Howard Inlet, Will Smith, Нью-Йорк и шестилетняя дочь.*

**Было:**

> The 2016 American fantasy drama film directed by David Frankel and written by Allan Loeb follows Howard Inlet (Will Smith), a successful New York advertising executive who falls into deep depression after the tragic death of his six-year-old daughter. In his grief, he writes letters to abstract concepts—Love, Time, and Death—expressing his anger and sorrow. Concerned about his mental state, his colleagues—Whit Yardshaw (Edward Norton), Claire Wilson (Kate Winslet), and Simon Scott (Michael Peña)—hire three actors: Amy (Keira Knightley) as Love, Raffi (Jacob Latimore) as Time, and Brigitte (Helen Mirren) as Death, to personally respond to Howard's letters. Their aim is either to help him cope with his grief or to prove his incompetence in managing the company. As the story unfolds, each colleague confronts their own personal struggles: Whit attempts to reconnect with his daughter, Claire faces fertility issues, and Simon hides a terminal illness. Howard finds solace in a support group where he meets Madeleine (Naomie Harris), a woman who has also lost her daughter. Gradually, he begins to understand that even in the darkest moments, there can be "collateral beauty"—unexpected moments of meaning and beauty. The film explores themes of grief, healing, and human connection. Despite its star-studded cast and profound themes, the film received predominantly negative reviews from critics but grossed approximately $88.5 million worldwide against a budget ranging from $36 to $40.3 million.

**Стало:**

**Было:**

> A 1971 American comedy-drama film directed by Mike Nichols and written by Jules Feiffer, "Carnal Knowledge" explores the sexual and emotional lives of two men, Jonathan Fuerst (Jack Nicholson) and Sandy (Art Garfunkel), from their college days at Amherst in the late 1940s through their adulthood in the 1970s. Sandy, romantic and sensitive, begins dating Susan (Candice Bergen), but Jonathan, more cynical and aggressive, seduces her, leading to the end of their relationship. After college, Sandy marries Susan, but their marriage proves unsatisfying. Jonathan enters a relationship with Bobbie (Ann-Margret), an attractive but emotionally unstable woman, resulting in her depression and a suicide attempt. Over the years, Jonathan becomes increasingly detached, incapable of emotional intimacy, and ultimately resorts to scripted role-play with a prostitute (Rita Moreno) to achieve arousal. The film sparked controversy for its candid depiction of sexuality and was subject to legal challenges for obscenity, but in 1974, the U.S. Supreme Court ruled it did not violate community standards. Ann-Margret received an Academy Award nomination for Best Supporting Actress, while Jack Nicholson and Art Garfunkel earned Golden Globe nominations.​

**Стало:**

> Mike Nichols directed "Carnal Knowledge," working from a script by Jules Feiffer. The 1971 American comedy-drama charts the sexual and emotional lives of two men, Jonathan Fuerst (Jack Nicholson) and Sandy (Art Garfunkel), from their college years at Amherst in the late 1940s into adulthood in the 1970s. Sandy, romantic and sensitive, begins dating Susan (Candice Bergen), but Jonathan, more cynical and aggressive, seduces her, leading to the end of their relationship. After college, Sandy marries Susan, but their marriage proves unsatisfying. Jonathan enters a relationship with Bobbie (Ann-Margret), an attractive but emotionally unstable woman, resulting in her depression and a suicide attempt. Over the years, Jonathan becomes increasingly detached, incapable of emotional intimacy, and ultimately resorts to scripted role-play with a prostitute (Rita Moreno) to achieve arousal. The film sparked controversy for its candid depiction of sexuality and was subject to legal challenges for obscenity, but in 1974, the U.S. Supreme Court ruled it did not violate community standards. Ann-Margret received an Academy Award nomination for Best Supporting Actress, while Jack Nicholson and Art Garfunkel earned Golden Globe nominations.​

## `backdraft-o5bccvq2`

*Ряд был 15 слов, стало 5: подлежащим сделаны номинации, порядок категорий перевёрнут; сохранены «три», Academy Award, Best Sound, Best Sound Editing, Best Visual Effects, «Backdraft». ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 10 слов подряд («film directed by ron howard and written by gregory widen…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> A 1991 American action thriller film directed by Ron Howard and written by Gregory Widen. The story centers on two Chicago firefighter brothers: Stephen McCaffrey (Kurt Russell), a seasoned and headstrong lieutenant, and his younger brother Brian (William Baldwin), who struggles to prove himself in the shadow of his elder sibling. Despite their strained relationship, they work together in the same firehouse. Concurrently, a series of mysterious arson cases grips the city, leading fire investigator Donald Rimgale (Robert De Niro) to suspect that the perpetrator may be connected to the fire department. The investigation uncovers that the fires are linked to Ronald Bartel (Donald Sutherland), a former firefighter serving a prison sentence, whose motives are tied to corruption within the city's administration. The film showcases realistic firefighting scenes, including the dangerous phenomenon of backdraft, where a sudden influx of oxygen causes explosive combustion. The cast also includes Scott Glenn (John Adcox), Jennifer Jason Leigh (Jennifer Vait), Rebecca De Mornay (Helen McCaffrey), and Jason Gedrick (Tim Krizminski). "Backdraft" received three Academy Award nominations for Best Sound, Best Sound Editing, and Best Visual Effects. With a budget of $40 million, it grossed approximately $152.4 million worldwide, becoming one of the most successful firefighter-themed films. A sequel, "Backdraft 2," was released in 2019, featuring William Baldwin and Donald Sutherland reprising their roles.

**Стало:**

*Ряд был 15 слов, стало 6: одно предложение разбито на два, рекорд подан отрицанием вместо превосходной степени; сохранены признание критики, третье место 2021, $848 млн, «Barbie», 2023.*

**Было:**

> "Hi, Mom" is a 2021 Chinese comedy-drama directed and co-written by Jia Ling, marking her directorial debut and drawing from her personal experiences. The film follows Jia Xiaoling (Jia Ling), a young woman who, after the tragic death of her mother Li Huanying (Zhang Xiaofei) in 2001, is mysteriously transported back to 1981. In the past, she befriends her young mother, aiming to improve her life by setting her up with the promising young man Shen Guanglin (Shen Teng). However, her attempts to alter the future lead to unforeseen consequences, prompting her to reevaluate her relationship with her mother and understand the depth of her love and sacrifices. The film explores themes of family values, maternal love, and filial piety, blending elements of comedy and drama. "Hi, Mom" received critical acclaim and became the third highest-grossing film of 2021, earning over $848 million, and was the highest-grossing film by a solo female director until "Barbie" in 2023. The film was honored at the 36th China Popular Film Awards and the 19th China Huabiao Awards.

> A 2015 British-French biographical crime thriller directed by Brian Helgeland, based on John Pearson's book "The Profession of Violence: The Rise and Fall of the Kray Twins." The film tells the story of twin brothers Ronald and Reginald Kray (both portrayed by Tom Hardy), who were influential figures in London's criminal underworld during the 1960s. Reggie Kray, a former boxer, seeks to expand his influence in the city's criminal underworld. His brother Ronnie, suffering from paranoid schizophrenia, is confined to a psychiatric hospital, but Reggie manages to secure his release. Together, they begin to establish control over nightclubs and other illicit enterprises through violence and extortion. Reggie starts a romantic relationship with Frances Shea (Emily Browning), his driver's sister, and they soon marry. However, his criminal activities and Ronnie's aggressive behavior lead to tension in their relationship. The brothers strike a deal with the American Mafia, represented by Angelo Bruno (Chazz Palminteri), to expand their influence. Meanwhile, Detective Leonard "Nipper" Read (Christopher Eccleston) of Scotland Yard initiates an investigation into their activities, aiming to bring them to justice. The film intricately portrays the rise and fall of the Kray twins, their internal conflicts, and their impact on London's criminal world during that era.

Ronnie has paranoid schizophrenia and sits in a psychiatric hospital until Reggie gets him released. Side by side, the brothers take over nightclubs and other illegal ventures by beating and extorting their way in. What the film lays out in detail is the brothers' ascent, their collapse, the fights between them, and the mark they left on criminal London of that decade.

## `viva-zapata-ytu2vgxf`

*Ряд 15 слов сбит до 4; сохранены пять номинаций Академии, Anthony Quinn, Best Supporting Actor и признание критиков.*

> The 1952 American biographical Western film depicts the life of Mexican revolutionary Emiliano Zapata (Marlon Brando), from his peasant origins, rise to power in the early 1900s, to his death in 1919. Zapata joins a delegation of peasants appealing to President Porfirio Díaz (Fay Roope) to reclaim lands taken by large landowners. After Díaz dismisses their pleas, Zapata, along with his brother Eufemio (Anthony Quinn), initiates a rebellion, aligning with Pancho Villa (Alan Reed) and the naive reformer Francisco Madero (Harold Gordon). Following Díaz's overthrow, Madero becomes president but fails to fulfill promises of land redistribution. Zapata grows disillusioned with the new government, especially as Madero entrusts power to General Victoriano Huerta (Frank Silvera), who betrays and murders Madero. Zapata continues his fight for peasant rights, facing betrayal and corruption, including abuses of power by his own brother Eufemio. Ultimately, Zapata is ambushed and killed, but his legacy continues to inspire peasants to fight for their rights. The film received critical acclaim and was nominated for five Academy Awards, with Anthony Quinn winning for Best Supporting Actor.

## `prince-of-the-city-zpvzcssg`

*Ряд 15 слов сбит до 2; сохранены самоубийство напарника на допросе, кузен в мафии, помощь Ciello и его гибель. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 10 слов подряд («skimming money from criminals and supplying informants with …») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> An American epic neo-noir crime drama film from 1981, produced in the USA under the direction of director Sidney Lumet. The plot is based on real events from the life of New York City police officer Robert Leuci, whose prototype in the film became the character Daniel Ciello (Treat Williams). Ciello serves in the Special Investigative Unit (SIU) of the New York Police Department, an elite unit known as the "Princes of the City" due to their broad autonomy and minimal supervision. However, this freedom leads to corruption: officers engage in illegal activities, including skimming money from criminals and supplying informants with stolen drugs for resale. Ciello, who has a drug-addict brother and a cousin involved in organized crime, begins to experience remorse after an incident in which he beats a junkie to steal his heroin. He is invited to cooperate with internal investigations and federal prosecutors to expose corruption in the police. Agreeing to work undercover and wear a wire, Ciello seeks to avoid prosecution and ensure protection for himself and his family, but insists on not betraying his partners. However, his past misdeeds and connections to the criminal world complicate the situation. One of his partners commits suicide during interrogation, and his cousin in the mafia, who had been assisting Ciello, ends up dead. Admitting to three crimes committed in the SIU, Ciello perjures himself by denying many other offenses committed by him and his partners. Despite his declarations of loyalty, he ultimately gives up all his partners, most of whom turn away from him. As a result, the chief prosecutor decides not to press charges against Ciello, and he is reassigned as an instructor at the police academy.

**Стало:**

## `look-who-s-talking-too-1qfgp1f8`

*Ряд 14 слов сбит до 4; сохранены 1990, американская романтическая комедия, Amy Heckerling, название "Look Who’s Talking" дословно в кавычках, вторая часть франшизы.*

**Было:**

> The 1990 American romantic comedy, directed by Amy Heckerling, is the sequel to "Look Who’s Talking" and the second installment in the franchise. The story follows Mollie (Kirstie Alley) and James Ubriacco (John Travolta) as they prepare for the arrival of their second child. Their son Mikey (voiced by Bruce Willis) eagerly anticipates the birth of his baby sister, imagining himself as her protector. However, when Julie (voiced by Roseanne Barr) is born, Mikey finds himself struggling with jealousy as his parents’ attention shifts away from him. Julie, rather than being the sweet little sister he imagined, is feisty and strong-willed, leading to frequent clashes between the siblings. Mikey also faces the challenges of potty training, often getting misguided advice from his mischievous friend Eddie (voiced by Damon Wayans). Meanwhile, James starts a new job as an airline pilot, causing tension in his marriage with Mollie due to his long absences. Their relationship becomes even more strained when Mollie’s younger brother, Stuart (Elias Koteas), moves in with them, adding to the chaos. As parental conflicts intensify, Mikey realizes his family is on the verge of falling apart and takes it upon himself to restore harmony. In the end, through love and understanding, the family comes together, recognizing the importance of supporting each other despite life’s difficulties. The film humorously explores themes of sibling rivalry, parenting struggles, and the value of family, showing how love can overcome even the toughest challenges.

**Стало:**

## `more-american-graffiti-25jgey5b`

*Ряд 14 слов сбит до 5; find расширен за ложную границу предложения на "Bill L.", ряд шёл дальше в "Norton". Сохранены 1979, Bill L. Norton, 1973, "American Graffiti", четыре кануна Нового года, 1964–1967.*

**Было:**

> The 1979 American coming-of-age comedy film, written and directed by Bill L. Norton, serves as a sequel to the 1973 film "American Graffiti" and chronicles the lives of the original characters over four consecutive New Year's Eves from 1964 to 1967. Each year is depicted with a distinct visual style: the 1965 Vietnam sequences are shot on grainy Super 16 mm film to mimic war reportage; the 1966 segments, focusing on the hippie movement and peace protests, utilize split screens and multiple angles to convey a sense of chaos and layered storytelling. The narrative follows several characters: John Milner (Paul Le Mat) continues his drag racing career but meets a tragic end in a car accident in 1964; Terry "The Toad" Fields (Charles Martin Smith) serves in Vietnam, where he fakes his death to desert; Steve Bolander (Ron Howard) and his wife Laurie (Cindy Williams) face marital challenges due to Laurie's feminist aspirations; Debbie Dunham (Candy Clark) transforms from a high school beauty into a hippie and becomes part of a musical band. The film addresses significant social issues of the 1960s, including the Vietnam War, the peace movement, women's liberation, and cultural shifts of the era. Despite the return of most original cast members, except Richard Dreyfuss, and an experimental narrative approach, the film received mixed reviews from critics and audiences, failing to replicate the success of its predecessor.

**Стало:**

## `the-platform-3hmkyofi`

*Ряд 14 слов через два предложения сбит до 3; сохранены 2019, испанский дистопичный триллер, Galder Gaztelu-Urrutia, промышленная башня, название "Vertical Self-Management Center" дословно, многоуровневая тюрьма.*

**Было:**

> A 2019 Spanish dystopian thriller directed by Galder Gaztelu-Urrutia. The film is set in a large industrial tower called the "Vertical Self-Management Center," where inmates are held in a multi-level prison. Each month, prisoners are moved between floors, and their feeding is managed by a vertically moving platform initially laden with an abundance of food. The platform gradually descends through the tower's levels, stopping at each floor for a fixed time. As the inhabitants of the upper floors consume as much as they can, those below receive progressively less, leading to conflicts and a struggle for survival. The story centers on Goreng (Iván Massagué), who voluntarily agrees to stay in the center to quit smoking and read a book. He awakens on Level 48 with cellmate Trimagasi (Zorion Eguileor), who explains the system's rules. As the plot unfolds, Goreng encounters various characters, including Imoguiri (Antonia San Juan), a former administrator of the center, and Baharat (Emilio Buale), an inmate striving to ascend to the upper levels. The film explores themes of social inequality, human nature, and survival under extreme conditions, offering a grim perspective on resource distribution and class disparities.

**Стало:**

The film tells the story of an unusual alliance between a gangster and a police officer who team up to catch a serial killer. Despite sustaining serious injuries, Dong-soo survives, making him the only witness capable of identifying the perpetrator. Detective Jung Tae-seok (Kim Mu-yeol), known for his relentless stance against crime, has long suspected the existence of a serial killer but lacks evidence. Realizing that collaborating with the gangster is the only way to catch "K," Tae-seok proposes an alliance to Dong-soo. Despite mutual distrust, they form a temporary partnership: the cop seeks justice, while the gangster desires revenge and the restoration of his reputation. Together, they embark on a hunt for the killer, facing obstacles from both the law and the criminal underworld. The film explores the complex relationship between law enforcement and crime, demonstrating how a common goal can unite even the most unlikely adversaries. In South Korea the film reached cinemas on May 15, 2019, and the 2019 Cannes Film Festival also showed it in the "Midnight Screenings" section, out of competition. > The 2014 Australian science fiction thriller directed by Michael and Peter Spierig is based on the 1959 short story “All You Zombies” by Robert A. Heinlein. The story follows a time-traveling agent (Ethan Hawke) who works for a covert government program designed to prevent crimes before they occur. The plot begins with the agent suffering severe burns during a failed attempt to stop a bombing by the elusive “Fizzle Bomber.” After undergoing reconstructive surgery, he is sent on one final mission. Disguised as a bartender in 1970s New York, he meets a mysterious customer who writes confession stories under the name “The Unmarried Mother.” Over drinks, the customer reveals a life story that begins with growing up as a girl named Jane in an orphanage, being recruited for a space program, falling in love with a mysterious man, and then becoming pregnant. Following childbirth, Jane is told that due to a rare medical condition, she must undergo gender reassignment and becomes John. The child is stolen, and John spends the next years adrift. As the story unfolds, time loops and paradoxes reveal that the bartender, Jane/John, and the child are all the same person—each version of the character traveling through time, creating a closed loop of existence. The film delves into themes of identity, fate, self-creation, and loneliness, offering a profound narrative that blurs the lines between protagonist and antagonist, past and future, love and betrayal, all within a tightly structured temporal paradox.

**Стало:**

> A 2009 American biographical sports drama directed by Clint Eastwood, with a screenplay by Anthony Peckham, based on John Carlin's book "Playing the Enemy: Nelson Mandela and the Game That Made a Nation." The film focuses on events in South Africa following the end of apartheid and the election of Nelson Mandela (Morgan Freeman) as president. Seeking to unite a divided nation, Mandela uses the 1995 Rugby World Cup, hosted in South Africa, as a means to achieve this goal. He approaches the national team's captain, Francois Pienaar (Matt Damon), urging him to inspire the team to victory despite skepticism and internal divisions. The film details how sport becomes a tool for national unity, overcoming racial prejudices, and building a new national identity. The cast also includes Tony Kgoroge, Patrick Mofokeng, Julian Lewis Jones, Adjoa Andoh, and Marguerite Wheatley. With a budget of $50–60 million, the film grossed over $122 million worldwide and received positive reviews from critics, particularly for the performances of Freeman and Damon, who were nominated for Academy Awards for Best Actor and Best Supporting Actor, respectively.

**Было:**

> The 2019 American science fiction action comedy "Men in Black: International" serves as the fourth installment in the "Men in Black" franchise and acts as a spin-off from the original trilogy. Directed by F. Gary Gray and written by Art Marcum and Matt Holloway, the film stars Chris Hemsworth as Agent H, Tessa Thompson as Agent M, Rebecca Ferguson as Riza Stavros, Kumail Nanjiani as the voice of Pawny, Rafe Spall as Agent C, Liam Neeson as High T, and Emma Thompson reprising her role as Agent O. The narrative follows Molly Wright (Tessa Thompson), who, after witnessing the Men in Black in action during her childhood, becomes determined to join the secret organization. Successfully locating the MIB headquarters in New York City, she convinces Agent O (Emma Thompson) to recruit her as a probationary agent under the alias Agent M. Assigned to the London branch, she partners with the charismatic yet reckless Agent H (Chris Hemsworth). Together, they investigate a series of alien attacks linked to mysterious twin aliens (Les Twins) capable of manifesting as pure energy. As Agents M and H delve deeper into the case, they uncover the existence of a mole within the MIB organization, leading them on a global adventure to protect Earth from a new alien threat. Their journey takes them to various international locations, including Marrakesh, Italy, and Paris, encountering diverse alien species and advanced technologies. The film blends action, comedy, and science fiction elements, staying true to the franchise's legacy while introducing a fresh cast of characters. "Men in Black: International" received mixed reviews from critics; while the chemistry between Hemsworth and Thompson was praised, the plot and lack of originality were points of criticism. With a production budget ranging between $94 and $110 million, the film grossed approximately $253.9 million worldwide, marking a moderate commercial success.

**Стало:**

## `the-last-tycoon-ap1yz77g`

*Ряд 14 → 8: имена переставлены перед категорией, поэтому ряд обрывается на списке и не переходит в следующее предложение; сохранены Казан, номинация «Оскара», Best Art Direction, Джин Каллахан, Джек Т. Коллис, Джерри Уандерлич.*

**Было:**

> A 1976 American romantic film directed by Elia Kazan and written by Harold Pinter, based on F. Scott Fitzgerald's unfinished novel "The Last Tycoon." Set in 1930s Hollywood, the film follows Monroe Stahr (Robert De Niro), a young and talented studio executive modeled after real-life producer Irving Thalberg. Stahr is deeply immersed in his work, overseeing all aspects of film production, from script approval to casting decisions. His professional life becomes entangled with personal challenges when he encounters Kathleen Moore (Ingrid Boulting), a woman who bears a striking resemblance to his late wife. Their complex relationship is further complicated by Kathleen's engagement to another man. Concurrently, Stahr faces growing tensions with labor unions and communist movements in Hollywood, particularly embodied by union organizer Brimmer (Jack Nicholson), leading to conflicts with studio executives, including Pat Brady (Robert Mitchum), father of Cecilia Brady (Theresa Russell), who secretly harbors feelings for Stahr. The film delves into themes of power, loneliness, love, and moral compromises within the entertainment industry. Despite its star-studded cast, featuring Tony Curtis as Rodriguez and Donald Pleasence as George Boxley, the film received mixed reviews from critics and was a commercial disappointment, grossing $1.8 million against a $5.5 million budget. Nonetheless, Kazan's direction was recognized with an Academy Award nomination for Best Art Direction (Gene Callahan, Jack T. Collis, and Jerry Wunderlich). "The Last Tycoon" marked the final film in Kazan's directorial career, concluding his cinematic contributions with a work that reflects the inner workings and complexities of 1930s Hollywood.

**Стало:**

> A 1976 American romantic film directed by Elia Kazan and written by Harold Pinter, based on F. Scott Fitzgerald's unfinished novel "The Last Tycoon." The film delves into themes of power, loneliness, love, and moral compromises within the entertainment industry. Despite its star-studded cast, featuring Tony Curtis as Rodriguez and Donald Pleasence as George Boxley, the film received mixed reviews from critics and was a commercial disappointment, grossing $1.8 million against a $5.5 million budget. Even so, the production did draw an Academy Award nomination under Kazan: Gene Callahan, Jack T. Collis, and Jerry Wunderlich were cited for Best Art Direction. > A tactical role-playing game developed by Intelligent Systems and published by Nintendo for mobile devices. It is the first game in the Fire Emblem series released for smartphones. Players control a team of heroes engaged in turn-based battles on grid-based maps, designed for touch-screen gameplay. The game features characters from various Fire Emblem titles, which can be summoned through a gacha system. The campaign introduces an original story where heroes fight to save the kingdoms of Askr and Embla. In addition to story missions, the game offers limited-time events, a PvP mode, and challenge battles with unique conditions. Fire Emblem Heroes follows a free-to-play model with optional in-game currency purchases for faster character acquisition. The game received positive feedback for its strategic depth, vibrant animation, and extensive roster of characters from classic Fire Emblem games.

> Call of Duty: Black Ops 6 is a first-person shooter developed by Treyarch and Raven Software. It is the twenty-first installment in the Call of Duty franchise and the seventh in the Black Ops subseries, serving as a direct sequel to Call of Duty: Black Ops Cold War (2020). The single-player campaign is set in 1991, where players control agents Troy Marshall and Frank Woods, who are combating a secret organization called "The Pantheon" that has infiltrated the CIA leadership. The game features a single-player campaign, multiplayer mode, and a cooperative "Zombies" mode. The multiplayer mode includes 16 new maps and brings back features from previous games in the series, such as the "Prestige" system. The "Zombies" mode offers two new maps, continuing the story from Black Ops Cold War. A new feature is the omnidirectional movement mechanic, allowing players to run, jump, and slide in any direction. The game was released on October 25, 2024, for PlayStation 4, PlayStation 5, Windows, Xbox One, and Xbox Series X/S.

Their reunion is abruptly interrupted when a group of mercenaries led by Frederick Balzary (Chad Michael Murray) infiltrates the resort, seeking revenge against Robert for past grievances. Forced to retreat into a high-tech bunker known as the "Fortress," Robert and Paul must utilize their skills and resources to fend off the attackers. The film also features Kelly Greyson as Kate Taylor, Ser'Darius Blain as Ulysses, and Shannen Doherty as Brigadier General Barbara Dobbs. "Fortress" combines elements of intense thriller and dynamic action, delving into themes of family relationships, betrayal, and redemption.

## `the-firm-i34i29rl`

**Было:**

> The 1989 British made-for-television drama film, directed by Alan Clarke and written by Al Hunter Ashton for the BBC, centers on Clive Bissell, nicknamed "Bex" (Gary Oldman), a respectable estate agent who leads a football hooligan firm called the Inter City Crew (ICC), inspired by the real-life Inter City Firm associated with West Ham United. Bex aims to unite various rival firms into a single "National Firm" to assert dominance during the upcoming 1988 European Championship in West Germany. His ambitions face resistance from other leaders, notably Yeti (Phil Davis), head of the rival "Buccaneers" firm. Bex's personal life is strained as his wife (Lesley Manville) expresses concern over his violent lifestyle, especially after their young son injures himself with a knife left unattended. Undeterred, Bex continues his activities, driven by the thrill and sense of purpose they provide. The film culminates in a tragic conclusion when Bex is killed by Yeti during a brutal confrontation, yet his followers continue to revere him as a hero, vowing to fight in his memory. Notable for its minimal musical score, save for Dean Martin's "That's Amore" during the opening credits, the film has been acclaimed for its realistic portrayal of football hooliganism and is considered one of Gary Oldman's finest performances.

**Стало:**

The plot follows Jones and Davis as she is tasked with creating a fake moon landing in case his actual mission fails. The film premiered on July 8, 2024, at AMC Lincoln Square in New York City and was widely released on July 12, 2024. Initially planned for a streaming release on Apple TV+, it was moved to a theatrical release after positive early test screenings. > An American animated television series created by Bruce Willis and produced by Film Roman. Premiering on September 23, 1996, the series ran until May 26, 1997, encompassing 36 half-hour episodes. The plot centers around an 11-year-old boy named Bruno who becomes a top spy for the secret organization GLOBE. GLOBE contacts Bruno through his computer and special gadgets, unaware of his young age, as he uses an avatar of an adult man modeled after Bruce Willis. Bruno's close associates, such as Jarlsburg (voiced by Tony Jay) and Harris (voiced by Mark Hamill), are also unaware that GLOBE does not know Bruno's true age and assume the organization deliberately sends the boy on dangerous missions. The series combines elements of comedy, adventure, and spy genres, following Bruno as he balances his double life, keeping it hidden from his parents and friends. In one episode, for instance, he claims to be camping in the backyard while actually embarking on a secret mission. "Bruno the Kid" was acclaimed for its humor and unique take on the spy genre, becoming popular among viewers in the late 1990s.

## `the-verdict-2otkbpit`

*Ряд 22 слова → 5. Правлены оба накрытых предложения (ряд заходил во второе). Сохранены: 1982, Люмет, Мамет, Барри Рид, роман 1980 года, Фрэнк Гэлвин, Пол Ньюман, алкоголизм и неэтичные практики.*

**Было:**

> A 1982 American legal drama directed by Sidney Lumet and written by David Mamet, adapted from Barry Reed's 1980 novel of the same name. The film follows Frank Galvin (Paul Newman), a once-promising attorney whose career has deteriorated due to alcoholism and unethical practices. Now handling minor cases in Boston, Frank seeks an opportunity to redeem himself. His former mentor, Mickey Morrissey (Jack Warden), presents him with a seemingly straightforward medical malpractice case expected to settle out of court. The case involves a young woman who fell into a coma after anesthesia during childbirth at a Catholic hospital overseen by the Archbishop of Boston. Upon visiting her in the hospital and witnessing her condition, Frank decides to reject the proposed settlement and take the case to trial, aiming not only for justice for the victim but also to restore his own honor and professional reputation. This decision pits him against the powerful Catholic Church and their seasoned attorney, Ed Concannon (James Mason), renowned for his impeccable defense strategies. Frank faces numerous challenges: his key witness, nurse Kaitlin Costello Price (Lindsay Crouse), disappears; Judge Hoyle (Milo O'Shea) exhibits clear bias against him; and his personal demons of alcoholism and self-doubt continue to haunt him. During trial preparation, Frank becomes involved with Laura Fischer (Charlotte Rampling), a mysterious woman with whom he develops a romantic relationship. However, it is later revealed that Laura was hired by Concannon to spy on Frank, dealing him another blow. Despite the betrayal and the seemingly hopeless situation, Frank perseveres. In court, he presents compelling evidence of the doctors' negligence, including testimony that the anesthesiologist was intoxicated during the procedure. His emotional closing argument appeals to the jurors' conscience, urging them to deliver a just verdict. Ultimately, the jury rules in favor of the plaintiff, awarding a significantly larger sum than was offered in the pre-trial settlement. The film concludes with Frank, having abstained from alcohol, sitting in his office, reflecting on the events and the possibility of a new beginning. "The Verdict" received critical acclaim and was nominated for five Academy Awards, including Best Picture, Best Director, and Best Actor for Paul Newman, whose profound and nuanced performance is considered one of the highlights of his career.

**Стало:**

## `billie-eilish-col96cs8`

*Ряд 22 слова → 6 (только название EP); сохранены 2015, возраст 13, «Ocean Eyes», Финнеас О'Коннелл как автор и продюсер, 2017, дебютный EP «Don't Smile at Me», топ-15 и все четыре страны.*

**Было:**

> American singer and songwriter, born December 18, 2001, in Los Angeles, USA. In 2015, at the age of 13, she gained public attention with the song "Ocean Eyes," written and produced by her brother Finneas O'Connell. In 2017, she released her debut EP "Don't Smile at Me," which reached the top 15 in charts across the US, UK, Canada, and Australia. Her debut studio album "When We All Fall Asleep, Where Do We Go?" (2019) topped the Billboard 200 and UK Albums Chart, with the single "Bad Guy" becoming the first track by an artist born in the 21st century to top the Billboard Hot 100. In 2020, Eilish became the first woman to win all four major Grammy categories in a single year: Best New Artist, Record of the Year, Song of the Year, and Album of the Year. She won an Academy Award in 2022 for the James Bond theme "No Time to Die" and a second Oscar in 2024 for "What Was I Made For?" from the film "Barbie." Her second album "Happier Than Ever" (2021) and third album "Hit Me Hard and Soft" (2024) received critical acclaim and commercial success. Eilish is known for her unique style blending pop, alternative pop, and electropop, as well as her active civic engagement, advocating for women's rights, environmental issues, and mental health awareness.

**Стало:**

*Ряд 22 слова → 3; сохранены 2017, американский фильм, чёрная комедия и криминал, режиссёр Джордж Клуни и все соавторы сценария (братья Коэн, сам Клуни, Грант Хеслов).*
