> A 2017 American black comedy crime film directed by George Clooney and co-written by the Coen brothers, Clooney, and Grant Heslov. Set in 1959, the story unfolds in the idyllic suburban community of Suburbicon. Gardner Lodge (Matt Damon) lives with his wife Rose and their son Nicky. Their peaceful life is shattered when two intruders break into their home, leading to Rose's death from a chloroform overdose. Rose's identical twin sister, Margaret (Julianne Moore), moves in to help but soon assumes Rose's identity and begins a relationship with Gardner. It is revealed that Gardner orchestrated the home invasion to murder Rose and collect on her life insurance policy. Meanwhile, the arrival of the African-American Mayers family in the neighborhood incites racial tensions among the white residents, diverting attention from Gardner's crimes. Insurance investigator Bud Cooper (Oscar Isaac) suspects foul play and attempts to blackmail Gardner and Margaret, but Margaret poisons him, and Gardner finishes him off. As their plan unravels, Margaret tries to poison Nicky, but is killed by one of the original intruders, who also kills Uncle Mitch before being shot. Gardner returns home, finds the aftermath, and offers Nicky a choice to flee with him, but unknowingly consumes the poisoned sandwich meant for his son and dies. The film concludes with Nicky playing baseball with Andy, the son of the Mayers family, symbolizing a glimmer of hope amidst the chaos. Despite its ambitious themes, the film received mixed reviews and was a box office failure, grossing $12.8 million against a $25 million budget.​

**Стало:**

> The 1955 American historical drama film directed by Arthur Lubin is set in 11th-century England. King Edward the Confessor orders Saxon lord Leofric to marry a Norman woman, Yolanda, but Leofric refuses and is imprisoned. In jail, he meets Godiva, the sheriff of Coventry's sister, and they fall in love. Despite political tensions and the growing conflict between Saxons and Normans, they marry. Godiva becomes an active participant in advocating for her people's rights. In the film's climax, to demonstrate the unity of the Saxons and their resistance to Norman oppression, Godiva undertakes her famous nude ride through the streets of Coventry. The film stars Maureen O'Hara as Lady Godiva, George Nader as Lord Leofric, Victor McLaglen as Grimald, Rex Reason as Harold, Torin Thatcher as Lord Godwin, and Eduard Franz as King Edward. Notably, Clint Eastwood appears in one of his earliest roles as a Saxon. The film blends romance, political drama, and legend, offering a fictionalized account of the well-known historical myth.

## `cowspiracy-the-sustainability-secret-5bwyv7cn`

*Ряд 30 слов → 8 (осталось только само имя фестиваля): фестивали стали подлежащими, «12th annual» отцеплено от названия; 2015, 12, обе награды и оба фестиваля на месте. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 11 слов подряд («that animal agriculture is the primary source of greenhouse …») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> A 2014 American documentary film directed by Kip Andersen and Keegan Kuhn explores the impact of animal agriculture on the environment, addressing issues such as climate change, water usage, deforestation, and the creation of ocean "dead zones." The film also examines the policies of major environmental organizations, including Greenpeace, Sierra Club, Surfrider Foundation, Rainforest Action Network, and Oceana, regarding these issues. Throughout their investigation, the filmmakers discover that many of these organizations avoid discussing the environmental impact of animal agriculture, despite its significant contribution to global ecological problems. The documentary features interviews with various experts and activists, including physician Michael Klaper, former rancher and activist Howard Lyman, author and journalist Michael Pollan, journalist Will Potter, among others. "Cowspiracy" raises questions about the transparency of environmental organizations and encourages viewers to consider the impact of their dietary choices on the planet. The film received recognition at international film festivals, winning the Audience Choice Award at the 2015 South African Eco Film Festival and the Best Foreign Film Award at the 12th annual Festival de films de Portneuf sur l'environnement. However, some critics dispute the film's claims that animal agriculture is the primary source of greenhouse gas emissions, pointing to lower estimates in other scientific studies.

**Стало:**

> A 2022 American superhero film directed by Sean O'Reilly, based on the graphic novel by Grant Chastain. The story is set in San Tiburon, the world's most dangerous maximum-security prison designed to incarcerate supervillains and enhanced individuals. The facility is equipped with power inhibitors to neutralize the abilities of its inmates, ensuring control over the diverse and often dangerous population. The narrative begins with the arrival of Payback (Dan Payne), a zealous vigilante whose mission to hunt down enhanced humans leads to his incarceration at San Tiburon. His entry into the prison disrupts the existing balance of power among inmates and staff. Warden Devlin (Michael Rooker), known as "The Overseer," is the corrupt and ambitious head of San Tiburon. He is particularly interested in Julius "The Lobe" Loeb (Bruce Willis), a notorious inmate with immense intellect and telekinetic abilities. The Lobe is rumored to have a hidden fortune, and Devlin is determined to extract its location to fund his luxurious retirement. Among the inmates is Diego Diaz (Brennan Mejia), a new prisoner whose sole ability is heightened empathy. Navigating the treacherous environment of San Tiburon, Diego must choose his allies carefully to avoid becoming a pawn in the deadly games between inmates and the warden. As tensions escalate, alliances form and betrayals unfold. The power struggle between The Lobe and The Overseer intensifies, leading to a violent uprising within the prison. Amidst the chaos, Diego's empathetic abilities become crucial in mediating conflicts and seeking a path to survival. The film culminates in a confrontation that challenges the corrupt systems in place, with characters forced to confront their own moral compasses. The resolution leaves the future of San Tiburon and its inhabitants hanging in the balance, questioning the true meaning of justice and redemption.

**Стало:**

> A 2022 American superhero film directed by Sean O'Reilly, based on the graphic novel by Grant Chastain. The story is set in San Tiburon, the world's most dangerous maximum-security prison designed to incarcerate supervillains and enhanced individuals. The facility is equipped with power inhibitors to neutralize the abilities of its inmates, ensuring control over the diverse and often dangerous population. The narrative begins with the arrival of Payback (Dan Payne), a zealous vigilante whose mission to hunt down enhanced humans leads to his incarceration at San Tiburon. His entry into the prison disrupts the existing balance of power among inmates and staff. Warden Devlin (Michael Rooker), known as "The Overseer," is the corrupt and ambitious head of San Tiburon. He is particularly interested in Julius "The Lobe" Loeb (Bruce Willis), a notorious inmate with immense intellect and telekinetic abilities. The Lobe is rumored to have a hidden fortune, and Devlin is determined to extract its location to fund his luxurious retirement. Brennan Mejia plays Diego Diaz, an inmate who has just arrived and whose only gift is heightened empathy. Every ally he picks in San Tiburon either keeps him alive or turns him into a pawn in the deadly games the warden and the prisoners play against one another. Loyalties shift, betrayals surface, and the rivalry between The Lobe and The Overseer boils over into a violent uprising inside the walls. In that chaos his empathy turns into the one useful skill: he mediates between the men around him and looks for a way to stay alive. Everything converges on a single confrontation that drags the corruption running the place into the open and forces each character to face his own moral compass. The ending settles nothing about San Tiburon or the people locked in it, and leaves the meaning of justice and redemption an open question.

> A Belgian-Dutch coming-of-age film directed by Anthony Schatteman in 2024. A heartfelt conversation with his grandfather, who shares a story of his enduring love for his late wife, inspires Elias to confront his fears. Realizing that love is too precious to let slip away, he gathers the courage to reconcile with Alexander, taking a brave step toward embracing his feelings and the possibility of love.

> A Belgian-Dutch coming-of-age film directed by Anthony Schatteman in 2024. The story follows 14-year-old Elias (Lou Goossens), living in the Belgian countryside, who befriends his new neighbor Alexander (Marius De Saeger), a confident boy from Brussels. As they grow closer, Alexander reveals he was in love with a boy and asks Elias if he has experienced true love, prompting Elias to reflect on his own feelings. For the first time, Elias begins to understand the depth of his emotions as he falls for Alexander. Fear of judgment and rejection wins out, though, and Elias denies what he feels, pulling away from Alexander, from his friends and from his own family to keep the truth hidden. His grandfather is the one who shifts something, telling him about the love he still carries for his late wife; that story gives Elias the nerve to face his fear. Released in China on October 1, 2024, the film grossed over ¥261 million (approximately $36.8 million) at the box office. However, it received mixed reviews from critics: James Marsh of the South China Morning Post gave it 1 out of 5 stars, calling it a "tedious trudge through lazily choreographed fight sequences, sluggish chases, and torridly scripted interactions between Chan’s lethargic protagonist and an interchangeable ensemble of goofball mercenaries." Paul Bramhall of City on Fire was similarly unimpressed, stating, "A charmless affair devoid of any purpose or entertainment value; at one point a bad guy says, 'What is Jackie Chan doing here!?', which is the same question anyone who dares watch it will likely also find themselves asking." Nevertheless, some reviewers found redeeming qualities; Mike McGranaghan of The Aisle Seat described it as an "infectiously silly" and "fast-paced comic adventure," despite "a couple wrong notes."

> A 2023 British biographical war drama film directed by Ellen Kuras in her feature directorial debut. The screenplay, written by Liz Hannah, John Collee, and Marion Hume, is based on the 1985 biography "The Lives of Lee Miller" by Antony Penrose. The film portrays the life of Lee Miller (Kate Winslet), a renowned photojournalist and model who served as a war correspondent for Vogue during World War II. The narrative follows her journey from modeling to war photography, her personal and professional relationships, and her struggle with inner demons post-war. The cast also includes Marion Cotillard as Solange d'Ayen, Andrea Riseborough as Audrey Withers, Andy Samberg as David Scherman, Alexander Skarsgård as Roland Penrose, and Josh O'Connor as Antony Penrose. The film premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival on September 9, 2023, and was released in the United Kingdom on September 13, 2024. It received positive reviews from critics, particularly praising Kate Winslet's performance, and grossed $23.5 million worldwide.

The crew includes First Officer and Captain's ex-wife Kelly Grayson (Adrianne Palicki), Dr. Claire Finn (Penny Johnson Jerald), pilot Gordon Malloy (Scott Grimes), security officer Alara Kitan (Halston Sage), Lieutenant Commander John LaMarr (J. Lee), officer Bortus (Peter Macon), and artificial intelligence Isaac (Mark Jackson). > A 2016 British romantic drama directed by Thea Sharrock, based on the novel of the same name by Jojo Moyes. The film follows Louisa "Lou" Clark (Emilia Clarke), a cheerful and quirky young woman from a small town who, after losing her job at a café, becomes a caregiver for Will Traynor (Sam Claflin), a young successful banker left paralyzed after an accident. Initially, Will is cynical and distant, but gradually, a deep bond forms between him and Lou. She endeavors to show Will that life can still be full of joy and adventure, organizing various activities and trips for him. However, Will, experiencing constant physical pain and mourning the loss of his former life, decides to pursue euthanasia at a Swiss clinic. Lou tries to change his mind but ultimately respects his choice. The film explores complex themes of love, loss, and an individual's right to choose their fate. Filming took place in picturesque locations across the United Kingdom, including Pembroke Castle in Wales and Chenies Manor House in Buckinghamshire. Released on June 3, 2016, in the UK and the US, the film received mixed reviews from critics but was warmly received by audiences, grossing $208 million worldwide against a budget of $20 million.

Twenty-eight days after the outbreak, the protagonist Jim (Cillian Murphy), a bicycle courier, awakens from a coma in an abandoned London hospital. However, they soon discover that the military has its own plans for the survivors, leading to conflict and a struggle for survival. ## `big-eyes-fosgrijz`

*Ряд 16 слов → 6; сохранены 2014, Tim Burton, Scott Alexander, Larry Karaszewski, Margaret Keane, Amy Adams, 1950s, 1960s; одно предложение разбито на два.*

**Было:**

> The 2014 American biographical drama film directed by Tim Burton, written by Scott Alexander and Larry Karaszewski, centers on the life of artist Margaret Keane (Amy Adams), whose paintings of children with large eyes became a sensation in the 1950s and 1960s. After divorcing her first husband, Margaret moves to San Francisco with her daughter and meets the charming yet talentless painter Walter Keane (Christoph Waltz). They marry, and Walter begins to claim credit for Margaret's distinctive artwork, presenting it as his own. His savvy marketing skills lead to widespread popularity, with reproductions selling in massive quantities. Under pressure and threats from Walter, Margaret continues to paint in isolation, concealing the truth even from her daughter. Eventually, she recognizes the injustice of the situation and, after relocating to Hawaii, publicly asserts her authorship. This leads to a court case where the judge orders both to create a painting in the courtroom. Walter refuses, citing a sore arm, while Margaret completes her piece, proving her authorship. The film delves into themes of gender inequality, manipulation, and the struggle for recognition, while also questioning the value of art and the importance of truth in creativity.

**Стало:**

> A 2019 American comedy-drama film directed by Richard Linklater, based on Maria Semple's novel of the same name. The film follows Bernadette Fox (Cate Blanchett), a talented architect suffering from agoraphobia, living in Seattle with her husband Elgie Branch (Billy Crudup) and their 15-year-old daughter Bee (Emma Nelson). After her award-winning house was demolished, Bernadette becomes depressed and reclusive, avoiding social interactions, including with other parents at Bee's school and especially their neighbor Audrey Griffin (Kristen Wiig). Bee requests a family trip to Antarctica as a reward for good grades, to which her parents agree. As the trip approaches, Bernadette's behavior becomes increasingly erratic: she considers using various prescription drugs to manage her anxiety and insomnia. Audrey insists that Bernadette remove an invasive blackberry plant from the hillside above her house, leading to conflict between them. The situation escalates when Bernadette disappears without a trace, prompting Elgie and Bee to search for her, uncovering hidden aspects of her life and trying to understand the reasons behind her disappearance. The film explores themes of family, personal identity, and self-fulfillment, illustrating how past traumas and unfulfilled ambitions can impact the present.

> A 2016 American political action thriller directed by Babak Najafi and written by Creighton Rothenberger, Katrin Benedikt, Chad St. John, and Christian Gudegast, serving as a sequel to "Olympus Has Fallen" (2013) and the second installment in the "Has Fallen" film series. The plot unfolds in London during the funeral of the British Prime Minister, attended by Western world leaders, including U.S. President Benjamin Asher (Aaron Eckhart). A terrorist organization led by arms dealer Aamir Barkawi (Alon Moni Aboutboul) orchestrates a massive attack, targeting key landmarks and attempting to assassinate the assembled leaders. Secret Service agent Mike Banning (Gerard Butler) becomes the sole protector of the president, striving to ensure his safety amidst the chaos. The film also features Morgan Freeman as Speaker of the House Allan Trumbull, Angela Bassett as Secret Service Director Lynne Jacobs, Robert Forster as General Edward Clegg, and Melissa Leo as Secretary of Defense Ruth McMillan. ## `william-shakespeare-s-julius-caesar-cuyt86se`

*Ряд 17 слов сбит до 6 (только само название номинации); сохранены пять номинаций, Academy Awards, Best Picture, Best Actor, Brando и победа в Best Art Direction – Black-and-White с тем же тире.*

**Было:**

> The 1953 American film, an adaptation of William Shakespeare's play, depicts the political intrigues and tragic events surrounding the assassination of Roman dictator Julius Caesar (Louis Calhern). After defeating Pompey, Caesar returns to Rome, where his growing popularity raises concerns among senators, particularly Cassius (John Gielgud) and Brutus (James Mason). Cassius persuades Brutus to join a conspiracy aimed at preventing Caesar from becoming a tyrant. Despite warnings from his wife Portia (Deborah Kerr) and omens of impending danger, Brutus decides to participate in the plot. On the day of the Senate meeting, ignoring the fears of his wife Calpurnia (Greer Garson), Caesar proceeds to the Senate, where he is assassinated by the conspirators. Following the murder, Mark Antony (Marlon Brando), a loyal supporter of Caesar, is granted permission to speak at the funeral. In his famous oration, he uses sarcasm and rhetorical devices to turn the public against the conspirators, inciting a civil war. In the ensuing battles, the forces of Antony and Octavius defeat those of Brutus and Cassius. Realizing the inevitability of their defeat, Cassius and Brutus commit suicide. The film received critical acclaim for Joseph L. Mankiewicz's direction and the performances, particularly Marlon Brando's portrayal of Mark Antony. It was nominated for five Academy Awards, including Best Picture and Best Actor for Brando, winning Best Art Direction – Black-and-White. Both Brando and Gielgud also received BAFTA Awards for their performances.

**Стало:**

Jackson), the nephew (later retconned as son in the 2019 film) of the legendary detective John Shaft (Richard Roundtree). The plot begins in 1998 when Shaft investigates the brutal assault of Trey Howard (Mekhi Phifer) outside a restaurant. He arrests Walter Wade Jr. (Christian Bale), the son of a wealthy real estate tycoon, after noticing blood on him. Wade claims self-defense. A waitress, Diane Palmieri (Toni Collette), witnesses the incident but refuses to testify. During the investigation, Shaft discovers that Wade racially harassed Trey, leading to the altercation. > A 2022 American drama film directed by Darren Aronofsky and written by Samuel D. Hunter, based on his play of the same name. The story follows Charlie (Brendan Fraser), a reclusive and severely obese English teacher who conducts online classes while keeping his webcam off, ashamed of his appearance. Charlie struggles with deep guilt after abandoning his wife and daughter years ago for a relationship with a man who later passed away, leading him into a cycle of binge eating and self-destruction. His only close companion is Liz (Hong Chau), a nurse and the sister of his deceased partner, who takes care of him but is powerless to change his fate. As his health deteriorates, Charlie makes a desperate attempt to reconnect with his estranged 17-year-old daughter, Ellie (Sadie Sink), a rebellious and resentful teenager who harbors deep anger toward him. He offers to help her with her essays, hoping to prove he still has value in her life. Meanwhile, a young missionary, Thomas (Ty Simpkins), visits Charlie, believing he can save him through faith, though he himself is running from his own troubled past. As Charlie's condition worsens, he strives to show Ellie that she is an amazing person despite her cynicism and hostility toward the world. The film delves into themes of redemption, self-destruction, forgiveness, and love, delivering a deeply emotional narrative highlighted by Brendan Fraser's Academy Award-winning performance.

The Resistance sends back a less advanced, reprogrammed T-800 Terminator to protect Connor and ensure the future of humanity. The center of events is the battle for survival between the almost extinct humanity and artificial intelligence, which is Skynet. Skynet is positioned as the US computer system "Global Digital Defense Network", developed by Cyberdyne Systems, which becomes independent and uncontrollable. ## `exo-pshzrvoq`

*Ряд 15 слов сведён к 3 («d o kai»); сохранены 2011, 2012, SM Entertainment и все девять имён (порядок алфавитный), оборот «consists of» убран.*

> South Korean-Chinese male music group formed in 2011 by SM Entertainment and debuting in 2012, consists of nine members: Xiumin, Suho, Lay, Baekhyun, Chen, Chanyeol, D.O., Kai, and Sehun. Initially divided into two sub-units: EXO-K, targeting the Korean audience, and EXO-M, targeting the Chinese audience. After the departure of members Kris, Luhan, and Tao in 2014–2015, the group continued as a single entity. EXO's musical style encompasses elements of K-pop, R&B, hip-hop, EDM, electropop, and dance-pop. Their debut mini-album "Mama" (2012) earned them early accolades, including Best New Asian Artist at the Mnet Asian Music Awards. The first studio album "XOXO" (2013) achieved commercial success, with the single "Growl" receiving critical acclaim. EXO became the first artists debuting in the 21st century to surpass 10 million album sales. By 2023, they became "septuple million sellers," with over one million copies sold for each of seven different albums. The group has received over 180 awards, including 23 Daesang, and was recognized as the most influential celebrity in South Korea by Forbes in 2015 and 2016. EXO is also known for their sub-units, such as EXO-CBX (Xiumin, Baekhyun, Chen) and EXO-SC (Sehun, Chanyeol), as well as individual members' achievements in music, film, and television.

> A 2004 American animated Christmas fantasy film directed by Robert Zemeckis, who also co-wrote the screenplay with William Broyles Jr. The film is based on the 1985 children's book of the same name by Chris Van Allsburg. Tom Hanks stars in multiple roles, including the Conductor and Santa Claus. The story follows a young boy who, on Christmas Eve, boards a magical train bound for the North Pole to meet Santa Claus. The film was the first full-length feature to be entirely created using motion capture technology, allowing for highly realistic animation. It premiered on October 13, 2004, at the Chicago International Film Festival and was released in the United States on November 10, 2004. With a budget of $165–170 million, the film grossed $318.2 million worldwide. The music was composed by Alan Silvestri, and the song "Believe," performed by Josh Groban, was nominated for an Academy Award for Best Original Song and won a Grammy Award in 2006. The film received mixed reviews from critics but has since become a beloved Christmas classic among audiences of all ages.

> Is a 2009 epic science fiction film co-produced, co-edited, written, and directed by James Cameron. It is set in the 2154, when humans are colonizing Pandora, a lush habitable moon of a gas giant in the Alpha Centauri star system, in order to mine the valuable mineral unobtanium — the room-temperature superconductor mineral. The expansion of the mining colony threatens the continued existence of a local tribe of Na'vi — a humanoid species indigenous to Pandora. > A 2012 American crime action film directed by Jessy Terrero and written by L. Philippe Casseus, "Freelancers" follows Jonas "Malo" Maldonado (50 Cent), the son of a slain NYPD officer, who enrolls in the police academy with his two best friends. Upon graduation, he is taken under the wing of his father's former partner, Captain Joe Sarcone (Robert De Niro), and joins the Street Vice Crime Task Force. Malo soon discovers that Sarcone and his team are corrupt cops involved in criminal activities. Learning from the District Attorney's office that Sarcone murdered his father for turning state's evidence, Malo seeks revenge by framing Sarcone for a theft from mob boss Gabriel Baez (Pedro Armendáriz Jr.). To prove his loyalty to Baez, Malo reluctantly kills one of his friends and, with Baez's help, eliminates the corrupt officers responsible for his father's death. In the end, the DEA approaches Malo, urging him not to take Sarcone's place as Baez's right-hand man, leaving him to contemplate his future. The film also stars Forest Whitaker as Lieutenant Dennis Lureu, Dana Delany as Lydia Vecchio, Vinnie Jones as Sully, and Malcolm Goodwin as A.D. Valburn. Despite a budget of $13.5 million, the film grossed only $456,350 and received predominantly negative reviews for its predictable plot and lackluster execution.

> An American family tragicomedy released in 1983, directed, written, and produced by James L. Brooks, adapted from Larry McMurtry's 1975 novel of the same name. The film spans thirty years of the complex and emotionally charged relationship between widow Aurora Greenway (Shirley MacLaine) and her daughter Emma Greenway-Horton (Debra Winger). Aurora is an independent and strong-willed woman who seeks to control her daughter's life, leading to constant conflicts. Emma, desiring independence, marries college professor Flap Horton (Jeff Daniels) despite her mother's disapproval. Their marriage faces financial struggles and mutual infidelities. The couple relocates from Houston to Iowa and then to Nebraska, where they have three children. Meanwhile, Aurora embarks on a romantic relationship with her neighbor, retired astronaut Garrett Breedlove (Jack Nicholson), bringing new emotions and challenges. The family's life takes a turn when Emma is diagnosed with terminal cancer. In her final months, Emma and Aurora reconcile, overcoming past disagreements. Emma entrusts her mother with the care of her children, and Garrett returns to support Aurora during this difficult time. The film received critical acclaim and commercial success, grossing $165 million at the box office against a budget of $8 million. At the 56th Academy Awards, the film received 11 nominations and won 5 awards: Best Picture, Best Director (James L. Brooks), Best Actress (Shirley MacLaine), Best Adapted Screenplay, and Best Supporting Actor (Jack Nicholson).​

Rounding out the group is Kofi's little brother, Jarius "G-Baby" Evans — too young to take the field, so he becomes the assistant coach. Initially, Conor views coaching as a way to quickly earn money, but gradually he becomes genuinely attached to his charges and starts to care for them sincerely. He also grows closer to their teacher, Elizabeth Wilkes (Diane Lane), who helps him understand the importance of responsibility and mentorship. As the plot develops, the team faces various challenges, including neighborhood violence and personal issues of the players. Tragedy strikes when one of the boys is killed by a stray bullet during a gang shootout, which deeply shocks Conor and makes him reconsider his life priorities. Despite the grief, the team decides to continue participating in the championship and advances to the final game, demonstrating resilience and unity. The film received mixed reviews from critics but was warmly received by audiences, who particularly praised the emotional depth of the story and Keanu Reeves' convincing performance.

With the support of his wife, Oracene "Brandy" Price (Aunjanue Ellis), Richard navigates social and racial challenges, faces resistance from the tennis establishment, and employs unconventional training methods to pave the way for his daughters' success. > The 1993 American sitcom, based on the 1992 film of the same name, follows the Rockford Peaches, an all-female baseball team during World War II. CBS carried the series over two weeks in 1993, between April 10 and 24, and showed two further episodes on August 13, 1993; a total of six episodes were produced, one of which remained unaired. The cast included Sam McMurray as Coach Jimmy Dugan, Carey Lowell as Dottie Hinson, Christine Elise as Kit Keller, Tracy Nelson as Evelyn Gardner, Megan Cavanagh as Marla Hooch, Tracy Reiner as Betty Horn, Wendy Makkena as Mae Mordabito, and Katie Rich as Doris Murphy. Despite featuring some actors from the original film, such as Megan Cavanagh and Tracy Reiner, the series failed to replicate the movie's success and was canceled after one season. Episode plots featured Dottie's return to the team after her husband was called back into service, a game between the girls and Jimmy's former teammates, the team acquiring a chimpanzee as a new mascot, a date between Jimmy and Dottie, the team's participation in a dance marathon, and Jimmy hiring a new shortstop based on her looks. In 2020, Amazon Prime Video ordered a reboot of the series, which premiered in 2022, offering a new interpretation of the story with a focus on issues of race and sexuality.​

**Стало:**

## `exodus-gods-and-kings-v5jpjfxj`

*Ряд 28 слов сломан до 2: залог активный, оба списка переставлены; год 2014, Ридли Скотт, все 4 сценариста и все 7 актёров на месте.*

**Было:**

> A 2014 American biblical epic film directed and produced by Ridley Scott, with a screenplay by Adam Cooper, Bill Collage, Jeffrey Caine, and Steven Zaillian. The film stars Christian Bale, Joel Edgerton, John Turturro, Aaron Paul, Ben Mendelsohn, Sigourney Weaver, and Ben Kingsley. Based on the biblical Book of Exodus, it tells the story of Moses (Christian Bale), who, raised in the Egyptian royal court, discovers his true Hebrew heritage and receives a divine calling to lead the Israelites out of Egyptian bondage. The narrative follows Moses as he confronts Pharaoh Ramesses II (Joel Edgerton), demanding the liberation of his people. After Pharaoh's refusal, Egypt is struck by ten plagues, including the turning of water into blood, infestations of frogs and locusts, boils, and the death of the firstborns. These calamities compel Ramesses to release the Israelites, but he soon changes his mind and pursues them with his army. The climax features Moses, guided by God, leading his people through the parted waters of the Red Sea, which then collapse upon the Egyptian forces. The film delves into themes of faith, leadership, and deliverance, portraying Moses as a complex and conflicted character grappling with doubts and the immense responsibility of guiding his people. Visually, the film is notable for its grand battle sequences, detailed depiction of ancient Egypt, and realistic portrayal of the biblical plagues. However, it received mixed reviews from critics, who praised the visual effects and performances but criticized the screenplay, pacing, and deviations from the biblical narrative. Additionally, the film sparked controversy for casting predominantly white actors in major roles, leading to accusations of "whitewashing." Despite grossing $268.2 million against a budget of $140–200 million, it fell short of commercial expectations and was banned in Egypt and the United Arab Emirates due to "historical inaccuracies." Nonetheless, "Exodus: Gods and Kings" stands as a significant attempt to bring a biblical story to the screen using modern cinematic techniques and a star-studded cast.

> A German pop duo formed in 1983 in West Berlin by composer and producer Dieter Bohlen and vocalist Thomas Anders, they became one of Germany's most successful musical acts, selling over 200 million records worldwide. Their debut single "You're My Heart, You're My Soul" (1984) topped the charts in 35 countries and sold over 8 million copies. Subsequent hits like "You Can Win If You Want," "Cheri, Cheri Lady," "Brother Louie," "Atlantis Is Calling (S.O.S. for Love)," and "Geronimo's Cadillac" solidified their popularity across Europe, Asia, South America, and the Middle East. Between 1985 and 1987, they released two albums per year, selling 65 million records in three years. After disbanding in 1987, Bohlen founded the project Blue System, while Anders pursued a solo career. In 1998, the duo reunited, releasing the album "Back for Good," which topped the charts in 15 countries and sold 3 million copies in Europe. They continued to release successful albums, including "Alone" (1999), "Year of the Dragon" (2000), "America" (2001), "Victory" (2002), and "Universe" (2003). In 2003, the duo disbanded permanently following the release of the compilation "The Final Album." Modern Talking significantly influenced the development of Eurodisco and synth-pop genres, and their music remains popular in Eastern European countries, including Latvia.

**Стало:**

> "Maria" is a 2024 American biographical psychological drama film directed by Pablo Larraín and written by Steven Knight. The film is an international co-production between Italy, Germany, the United Kingdom, and the United States. It centers on the last seven days of opera singer Maria Callas's (Angelina Jolie) life in Paris in 1977, as she reflects on her life and career. The cast also includes Pierfrancesco Favino, Alba Rohrwacher, Haluk Bilginer, Stephen Ashfield, Valeria Golino, and Kodi Smit-McPhee. The main character, Ember Lumen (Leah Lewis), a fiery young woman, assists her parents, Bernie (Ronnie del Carmen) and Cinder Lumen (Shila Ommi), in running their shop, the Fireplace. Ember's parents emigrated to Element City and opened the shop to secure a future for their daughter. Ember is preparing to take over the family business, but her fiery temper hinders this transition. During one of her emotional outbursts in the shop's basement, a water leak occurs, attracting the attention of water inspector Wade Ripple (Mamoudou Athie). Wade reports the violations to the city department, which could lead to the shop's closure. To prevent this, Ember and Wade embark on a joint investigation to find the source of the leaks and save the shop. Along the way, they grow closer despite their elemental differences. The film explores themes of family, identity, and embracing differences. It premiered on May 27, 2023, at the closing of the 76th Cannes Film Festival out of competition, and was released in the United States on June 16, 2023, in RealD 3D, 4DX, and Dolby Cinema formats. > An American Christmas comedy-drama film released in 2023, directed by Alexander Payne and written by David Hemingson. The story is set in December 1970 at Barton Academy, a boarding school in New England. The strict and fastidious ancient history teacher, Paul Hunham (Paul Giamatti), disliked by students and colleagues for his harsh grading and unpleasant demeanor, is reprimanded by Headmaster Hardy Woodruff (Andrew Garman) after failing an important benefactor's son on an exam. As punishment, he is assigned to supervise five students remaining on campus during the Christmas holidays, including Angus Tully (Dominic Sessa), whose mother canceled his trip to spend her honeymoon with her new husband. Also staying at the school is the cook, Mary Lamb (Da'Vine Joy Randolph), mourning the loss of her son who died in Vietnam. Hunham imposes study and physical activities on the students even during the break. Six days later, a parent retrieves four students for a ski trip, leaving Angus, Hunham, and Mary at the academy. After an incident where Angus dislocates his arm, the relationship between him and Hunham begins to change. Hunham organizes a modest Christmas celebration, and they embark on an "educational trip" to Boston, visiting Mary's son's grave and spending time at a restaurant. The film received positive reviews from critics and grossed $43 million worldwide. It was listed among the top ten films of 2023 by the National Board of Review and the American Film Institute and received numerous awards, including two Golden Globe Awards and two BAFTA Awards. Da'Vine Joy Randolph was honored as Best Supporting Actress at both ceremonies. The film also garnered five Academy Award nominations, including Best Picture and Best Actor for Paul Giamatti.

> This 2023 Australian fantasy adventure comedy film, directed by Jeffrey Walker and written by Leon Ford, is based on Tom Holt's novel of the same name. The story follows Paul Carpenter (Patrick Gibson) and Sophie Pettingel (Sophie Wilde), lowly interns who begin working at the mysterious London firm J.W. Wells & Co. They soon discover that their employers are not conventional businessmen but charismatic villains: CEO Humphrey Wells (Christoph Waltz) and middle manager Dennis Tanner (Sam Neill), who are disrupting the magical world by integrating modern corporate strategies into ancient magical practices. As the plot unfolds, Paul and Sophie uncover the true agenda of the corporation and must confront the destructive plans of their superiors. The film also features Miranda Otto as Countess Judy, Rachel House as Nienke Van Spee, Jessica De Gouw as Rosie Tanner, and Chris Pang as Casimir. Combining elements of magic, humor, and adventure, the film explores themes of corporate greed, personal responsibility, and the power of friendship. It premiered in Australia on March 23, 2023, and became available for streaming on April 7 on platforms including Stan in Australia, Sky Cinema in the UK, and MGM+ in the US. The film received positive reviews from critics, who praised its original storyline, visual effects, and performances, particularly highlighting the roles of Waltz and Neill.

## `witness-hnph8hdk`

*Ряд 17 слов; разбил на два ядра и убрал связку «winning …» — сохранены восемь номинаций, Academy Award, Best Picture, Best Actor для Ford, обе победы; остаточный ряд 3 слова. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 11 слов подряд («1985 american neo noir crime thriller film directed by peter…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> The 1985 American neo-noir crime thriller film, directed by Peter Weir, follows Philadelphia detective John Book (Harrison Ford) as he investigates a murder witnessed by eight-year-old Amish boy Samuel Lapp (Lukas Haas). When it's revealed that the killer is corrupt police officer James McFee (Danny Glover), Book takes Samuel and his widowed mother, Rachel Lapp (Kelly McGillis), into protective custody. After an attempt on his life, Book hides them in their Amish community in Pennsylvania, where he must also conceal himself. As Book recuperates, he assimilates into the Amish way of life, participating in a barn raising and developing a romantic connection with Rachel, causing tension with her suitor, Daniel Hochleitner (Alexander Godunov). When the corrupt officers locate Book, a climactic confrontation ensues, with the community's support leading to their defeat. The film delves into themes of cultural clash, love, and moral choices, highlighting the contrast between urban violence and the Amish's peaceful philosophy. It received eight Academy Award nominations, including Best Picture and Best Actor for Ford, winning Best Original Screenplay and Best Film Editing.

**Стало:**

> The 1988 American crime thriller directed by Peter Hyams follows the investigation of a murder at the Presidio Army base in San Francisco. Military Police officer Patti Jean Lynch (Jenette Goldstein) is shot dead while investigating a break-in, and two San Francisco Police Department officers are killed during the getaway. Detective Jay Austin (Mark Harmon), a former Military Police officer, is assigned to the case and clashes with Lieutenant Colonel Alan Caldwell (Sean Connery), the base's provost marshal. Their strained relationship stems from past conflicts when Austin was demoted after arresting an officer for drunk driving. Despite their differences, they team up to investigate, leading them to Arthur Peale (Mark Blum), a wealthy businessman with CIA ties and a military background in Vietnam. The investigation uncovers a diamond smuggling operation involving water bottles transported from the Philippines. It is revealed that former military personnel, including Sergeant Major Ross Maclure (Jack Warden), a close friend of Caldwell, are involved in the scheme. The final confrontation results in a shootout where Peale and his associates are killed, and Maclure is fatally wounded. The film concludes with a military funeral, where Caldwell delivers an emotional eulogy, and a reconciliation occurs between Caldwell and his daughter Donna (Meg Ryan), who has developed a romantic relationship with Austin. The film combines elements of crime thriller and drama, exploring themes of honor, loyalty, and redemption.​

## `the-insider-odqwyebl`

*Ряд 17 слов на двух предложениях; поставил Манна подлежащим, вынес название статьи вперёд, разделил вторую фразу на две — 1999, Michael Mann, Eric Roth, Marie Brenner, 1996, Vanity Fair, Jeffrey Wigand, Russell Crowe, Brown & Williamson сохранены, название статьи оставлено дословно в кавычках (это и есть остаточный ряд 6 слов), прочие ряды 4 слова.*

**Было:**

> The 1999 American biographical drama film directed by Michael Mann and written by Mann and Eric Roth is based on Marie Brenner's 1996 Vanity Fair article "The Man Who Knew Too Much." The film tells the story of Jeffrey Wigand (Russell Crowe), a former vice president of research and development at the tobacco company Brown & Williamson, who becomes a whistleblower by revealing that the company deliberately manipulated the composition of cigarettes to increase their addictiveness. "60 Minutes" producer Lowell Bergman (Al Pacino) convinces Wigand to give an interview for the program despite pressure and threats from his former employer and legal obstacles. Throughout the preparation and aftermath of the interview, Wigand faces personal and professional challenges, including lawsuits, threats to his family, and attempts to discredit his reputation. The film provides a detailed depiction of the inner workings of investigative journalism, the ethical dilemmas faced by the media, and the influence of corporate interests on freedom of speech. Supporting characters include Mike Wallace (Christopher Plummer), the host of "60 Minutes," and Don Hewitt (Philip Baker Hall), the executive producer of the program, who become involved in the conflict between journalistic ethics and CBS's corporate interests. The film received critical acclaim for its direction, screenplay, and especially for Russell Crowe's performance, which earned him an Academy Award nomination for Best Actor. Despite its critical success, the film did not recoup its budget at the box office, grossing approximately $60 million against a budget of $68 to $90 million. Nevertheless, "The Insider" is considered one of the best films about investigative journalism and whistleblowers, raising important questions about freedom of speech, corporate responsibility, and journalistic ethics.​

**Стало:**

The film follows Joey Boca (Kevin Kline), a pizzeria owner in Tacoma, Washington, who habitually cheats on his devoted wife, Rosalie (Tracey Ullman). Upon discovering her husband's numerous infidelities, Rosalie conspires with her mother, Nadja (Joan Plowright), to murder Joey. They enlist the help of inept hitmen Harlan James (William Hurt) and Marlon James (Keanu Reeves), as well as involving the young pizzeria employee Devo Nod (River Phoenix), who secretly harbors feelings for Rosalie. Despite multiple attempts involving poisoning, beating, and even shooting, Joey miraculously survives each assassination effort. Eventually, the plot is uncovered, leading to the arrest of Rosalie and her accomplices. However, Joey, realizing his mistakes and his genuine love for his wife, forgives her, and they decide to preserve their marriage despite the tumultuous events. John Kostmayer built a screenplay out of that real case, and Lawrence Kasdan filmed it as a 1990 American black comedy. The cast he assembled runs deep: Kevin Kline and Tracey Ullman lead it, with Joan Plowright, William Hurt, Keanu Reeves, and River Phoenix around them. The film follows Joey Boca (Kevin Kline), a pizzeria owner in Tacoma, Washington, who habitually cheats on his devoted wife, Rosalie (Tracey Ullman). Upon discovering her husband's numerous infidelities, Rosalie conspires with her mother, Nadja (Joan Plowright), to murder Joey. They enlist the help of inept hitmen Harlan James (William Hurt) and Marlon James (Keanu Reeves), as well as involving the young pizzeria employee Devo Nod (River Phoenix), who secretly harbors feelings for Rosalie. Despite multiple attempts involving poisoning, beating, and even shooting, Joey miraculously survives each assassination effort. Eventually, the plot is uncovered, leading to the arrest of Rosalie and her accomplices. However, Joey, realizing his mistakes and his genuine love for his wife, forgives her, and they decide to preserve their marriage despite the tumultuous events. The film blends dark humor with an exploration of themes such as marital relationships, forgiveness, and redemption, offering audiences both comedic and poignant moments.

> The 2001 American drama film directed by Richard Linklater and written by Stephen Belber, based on his play of the same name, is a chamber piece unfolding entirely in real time within a motel room in Lansing, Michigan. The narrative centers on three former high school classmates: Vince (Ethan Hawke), a small-time drug dealer and volunteer firefighter; Jon Salter (Robert Sean Leonard), an aspiring documentary filmmaker visiting for a film festival; and Amy Randall (Uma Thurman), now an assistant district attorney. Vince arranges a meeting with Jon to revisit their shared past, particularly an incident from a decade earlier where, according to Vince, Amy accused Jon of rape. Through intense conversation, Vince coerces a confession from Jon, secretly recording it, and then invites Amy to join them to clarify the matter. Amy, however, asserts that her encounter with Jon was consensual and, to teach a lesson, pretends to call the police, warning the men to leave. The film delves into themes of memory, guilt, subjective perception, and moral responsibility, emphasizing psychological tension and character dynamics. Its minimalist setting and strong performances garnered positive critical reception, noted for its intensity and dramatic depth.

> A 1998 American science fiction disaster film directed by Mimi Leder, written by Bruce Joel Rubin and Michael Tolkin. Teenage astronomer Leo Biederman (Elijah Wood) and professional astronomer Dr. Wolf first detect the comet, but Dr. Wolf dies in a car accident shortly thereafter. Journalist Jenny Lerner (Téa Leoni) inadvertently uncovers a government conspiracy related to this impending danger. U.S. President Tom Beck (Morgan Freeman) announces a mission to destroy the comet using the spacecraft "Messiah," led by astronaut Spurgeon Tanner (Robert Duvall). The mission fails, splitting the comet into two fragments, both still headed toward Earth. As the catastrophe looms, the government constructs underground shelters for selected citizens, leading to public unrest. The film explores themes of sacrifice, human resilience, and moral dilemmas in the face of global crisis. The cast also includes Vanessa Redgrave, Maximilian Schell, and James Cromwell. With a budget of $80 million, the film grossed over $349 million worldwide, becoming the sixth highest-grossing film of 1998.

## `venus-vacation-prism-dead-or-alive-xtreme-neyr3n7z`

*Ряд 19 слов сломан до 6: сохранены 27 марта 2025, PlayStation 4, PlayStation 5, Microsoft Windows, Steam, DMM Games. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 17 слов подряд («any time during character episodes by sharing photos of the …») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> Venus Vacation Prism: Dead or Alive Xtreme (stylized as Venus Vacation PRISM) is an upcoming dating simulation video game developed by Team Ninja and published by Koei Tecmo. The game is scheduled for release on March 27, 2025, for PlayStation 4, PlayStation 5, and Microsoft Windows (via Steam and DMM Games). Similar to previous titles like Dead or Alive Xtreme 3 and Dead or Alive Xtreme Venus Vacation, this game will be available only in select Asian countries. Players assume the role of the manager of the Venus Islands, a tropical paradise where they interact with six female characters. Unlike previous entries where dating sim aspects were secondary, in Venus Vacation Prism, they are central to the story, which unfolds based on the player's choices, leading to multiple outcomes. A photo mode is accessible at any time during character episodes. By sharing photos of the participants in-game, a popularity gauge increases, advancing the Venus Festival. Once a certain popularity threshold is met, new outfits and hairstyles become available for the characters. The character roster includes: Misaki — a shy part-time staff member working alongside the manager. Fiona — a naive princess who fell for the manager at first sight. Elise — a beautiful yet strict supervisor sent to oversee activities on the Venus Islands. Tamaki — an alluring fashion designer and former model. Honoka — a laid-back schoolgirl with a secret (a character from the main Dead or Alive series). Nanami — a cool beauty visiting her childhood friend Misaki. The game was officially announced on September 27, 2024, by producer Yasunori Sakuda alongside voice actress Minami Tsuda. Asian versions of the game will support English subtitles. On November 1, 2024, the first trailer was released, confirming the final character lineup and an initial release date of March 6, 2025. However, on January 22, 2025, at the Taipei Game Show 2025, it was announced that the release date had been postponed to March 27, 2025, to allow for the addition of new episodes and quality improvements. The game is being developed using Koei Tecmo's in-house "Katana Engine".

**Стало:**

> "Alpha Dog" is a 2006 American crime drama film written and directed by Nick Cassavetes. The film is based on the true story of the kidnapping and murder of Nicholas Markowitz in 2000. It centers on Johnny Truelove (Emile Hirsch), a young drug dealer in the San Gabriel Valley, and his gang, including Frankie Ballenbacher (Justin Timberlake) and Elvis Schmidt (Shawn Hatosy). When Jake Mazursky (Ben Foster) fails to repay a debt to Johnny, Truelove and his accomplices kidnap Jake's younger brother, 15-year-old Zack Mazursky (Anton Yelchin), intending to use him as leverage to recover the debt. Initially, Zack views the situation as an adventure, befriending Frankie and his friends, but the situation escalates when Johnny decides to eliminate Zack to avoid legal repercussions. The film explores themes of youth crime, the consequences of poor decisions, and the devastating impact of violence. The cast also includes Bruce Willis as Sonny Truelove, Johnny's father, and Sharon Stone as Olivia Mazursky, Zack's mother. "Alpha Dog" premiered at the Sundance Film Festival on January 27, 2006, and was released in the United States on January 12, 2007. The film received mixed reviews from critics and grossed approximately $32 million worldwide against a budget of $9.5 million.

**Стало:**

**Было:**

> Indiana Jones and the Great Circle is an action-adventure video game developed by MachineGames and published by Bethesda Softworks in 2024. Based on the Indiana Jones franchise, the game features an original narrative inspired by the film series. Set in 1937, between the events of "Raiders of the Lost Ark" (1981) and "Indiana Jones and the Last Crusade" (1989), players control archaeologist Indiana Jones as he attempts to thwart various groups seeking to harness a power connected to the Great Circle—a series of mysterious sites around the world that form a perfect circle on a map. The story spans real-world locations such as the Vatican City, Thailand, Egypt, and China. The game is primarily played from a first-person perspective, with third-person elements employed for environmental interactions. Players navigate through a mix of linear, story-driven areas and wider exploratory landscapes. Combat can be approached directly or avoided using stealth mechanics. Indy's signature whip serves as both a weapon and a tool for traversing obstacles and solving puzzles, uncovering alternate paths and hidden collectibles. The game's development was announced in January 2021 by Bethesda, MachineGames, and Lucasfilm Games. Directed by Jerk Gustafsson, the game features an original score by Gordy Haab, who also reinterpreted classic themes composed by John Williams.

**Стало:**

You play Indiana Jones himself, working to stop rival groups from tapping a power tied to the Great Circle: a chain of strange sites scattered across the globe that line up as a perfect circle on a map. > The 2010 American war drama television series, produced in collaboration with HBO, Playtone, and DreamWorks, serves as a spiritual successor to the miniseries "Band of Brothers" and focuses on the actions of the United States Marine Corps in the Pacific Theater during World War II. The series follows the lives of three Marines: Robert Leckie (James Badge Dale), Eugene Sledge (Joseph Mazzello), and John Basilone (Jon Seda), who served in different regiments of the 1st Marine Division. Based on the memoirs "With the Old Breed" by Eugene Sledge and "Helmet for My Pillow" by Robert Leckie, as well as Chuck Tatum's recollections, the series covers key battles including Guadalcanal, Cape Gloucester, Peleliu, Iwo Jima, and Okinawa. It delves deeply into the psychological and emotional impacts of war, illustrating how combat affects soldiers and their families. The screenplay was written by Bruce C. McKenna, Robert Schenkkan, Graham Yost, George Pelecanos, Laurence Andries, and Michelle Ashford. The musical score was composed by Hans Zimmer, Geoff Zanelli, and Blake Neely. The series comprises 10 episodes and premiered on HBO on March 14, 2010.​

## `path-of-the-panther-rmqogvlr`

*Ряд 16 слов сбит до 4; переписан весь накрытый кусок из 4 предложений, сохранены оба упоминания 18 млн акров, Betty Osceola, Seminole Tribe, пять лет, камерные ловушки и Florida Wildlife Corridor Act.*

**Было:**

> "Path of the Panther" is a 2023 American documentary film focusing on the efforts to conserve the endangered Florida panther and its habitat in the Everglades. The film follows National Geographic photographer Carlton Ward Jr., who, along with a team of biologists, ranchers, and Indigenous community members, strives to prevent environmental degradation. The documentary emphasizes the importance of preserving the Florida Wildlife Corridor—a network of over 18 million acres of protected and private lands essential for the panther's survival and the region's biodiversity. It also features Betty Osceola, an environmental advocate from the Seminole Tribe, highlighting the cultural and ecological significance of protecting the panther's habitat. The production spanned nearly five years, employing innovative filming techniques such as camera traps to capture rare footage of the Florida panther in the wild. The film influenced the passage of the Florida Wildlife Corridor Act, aimed at safeguarding and connecting over 18 million acres of habitat. "Path of the Panther" premiered in select Florida theaters before becoming available on Disney+ in May 2023.

**Стало:**

> "Path of the Panther" is a 2023 American documentary film focusing on the efforts to conserve the endangered Florida panther and its habitat in the Everglades. The film follows National Geographic photographer Carlton Ward Jr., who, along with a team of biologists, ranchers, and Indigenous community members, strives to prevent environmental degradation. The film builds its case around the Florida Wildlife Corridor: more than 18 million acres, some publicly protected and some in private hands, that the panther needs to survive and the region needs for its biodiversity. Betty Osceola, an advocate for the environment from the Seminole Tribe, appears as well, and through her the film shows what the panther's habitat means culturally as much as ecologically. Crews worked for nearly five years and leaned on inventive methods, camera traps among them, to capture rare wild footage of the Florida panther. That effort fed the Florida Wildlife Corridor Act, which the film helped push through; the law sets out to shield more than 18 million acres of habitat and link them together. "Path of the Panther" premiered in select Florida theaters before becoming available on Disney+ in May 2023.

> A 1986 American science fiction comedy horror film directed by Stephen Herek in his directorial debut. The film follows a group of small, furry aliens known as Critters, who possess an insatiable appetite and aggressive behavior. These creatures escape from an intergalactic prison and head to Earth, landing in a small Kansas town. There, they begin terrorizing the Brown family: father Jay (Billy Green Bush), mother Helen (Dee Wallace), daughter April (Nadine van der Velde), and son Brad (Scott Grimes). The Critters have the ability to reproduce rapidly and cause chaos by devouring everything in their path. Following them to Earth are two intergalactic bounty hunters capable of changing their appearance, tasked with capturing and destroying the escaped convicts. One of the hunters assumes the form of rock star Johnny Steele (Terrence Mann), while the other, initially undecided on a form, later takes on the appearance of local drunk Charlie McFadden (Don Opper). The hunters team up with the Brown family and Charlie to confront the threat and save the town from the Critters' invasion. The film blends elements of comedy and horror, offering viewers an entertaining and suspenseful experience.

## `the-panic-in-needle-park-zckrdtyx`

*Ряд 16 слов сбит до 2; сохранены Bobby/Al Pacino, Helen/Kitty Winn, зависимость, мелкое мошенничество, беспокойность и обаяние. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 10 слов подряд («the best actress award at the 1971 cannes film festival…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> A 1971 American drama directed by Jerry Schatzberg. The film focuses on the lives of a group of heroin addicts who congregate in "Needle Park" (the nickname for Sherman Square on Manhattan's Upper West Side). The central characters are Bobby (Al Pacino), a young addict and small-time hustler, and Helen (Kitty Winn), a restless woman who finds Bobby charismatic. Initially, Helen does not use drugs, but under Bobby's influence, she becomes addicted to heroin. As their addictions deepen, their lives spiral downward, leading to a series of betrayals and personal tragedies. The film is noted for its raw and unflinching portrayal of drug addiction, which sparked discussions and controversies upon its release. This was Al Pacino's first leading role in a film, attracting the attention of director Francis Ford Coppola and leading to his subsequent casting in "The Godfather." Kitty Winn's portrayal of Helen earned her the Best Actress award at the 1971 Cannes Film Festival.

**Стало:**

## `the-replacements-htroc9z1`

*Ряд 16 слов сбит до 3; оба накрытых предложения перестроены и пересобраны в три, сохранены Washington Sentinels, Edward O'Neil (Jack Warden), Jimmy McGinty (Gene Hackman), четыре игры, три победы и плей-офф.*

**Было:**

> A 2000 American sports comedy directed by Howard Deutch. The story centers on a professional football league hit by a players' strike with four games left in the season. Washington Sentinels owner Edward O'Neil (Jack Warden) enlists former coach Jimmy McGinty (Gene Hackman) to lead the team through the remaining games, tasking him with winning three out of four to secure a playoff spot. Granted full autonomy in player selection, McGinty assembles a team of diverse and eccentric individuals. For quarterback, he chooses Shane Falco (Keanu Reeves), a former college football star whose career derailed after a disastrous Sugar Bowl performance. Now living on a houseboat and making a living cleaning yacht hulls, Falco is reluctant but eventually agrees. Other team members include Nigel Gruff (Rhys Ifans), a Welsh kicker with a gambling problem; Clifford Franklin (Orlando Jones), a speedy but clumsy wide receiver; Daniel "Bateman" Bateman (Jon Favreau), an aggressive former SWAT officer; and brothers Jamal (Faizon Love) and Andre Jackson (Michael Taliferro), former bodyguards. As the team faces various challenges on and off the field, a romantic relationship develops between Falco and the team's head cheerleader, Annabelle Farrell (Brooke Langton). The film explores themes of second chances, team spirit, and overcoming personal setbacks, blending humor with dynamic sports scenes to create an inspiring story about a group of underdogs striving for success.

**Стало:**

Jack Warden plays Edward O'Neil, owner of the Washington Sentinels, who brings back a former coach, Jimmy McGinty (Gene Hackman), to run the team through what remains of the season. He gives McGinty a single target: three wins out of those four, and the Sentinels take a playoff spot. ## `foyle-s-war-ox93icyl`

*Ряд 18 → 6 слов: порядок помощников перевёрнут, залог активный; сохранены Samantha «Sam» Stewart, Honeysuckle Weeks, Detective Sergeant Paul Milner, Anthony Howell, бывший солдат без ноги.*

**Было:**

> A British detective television series created by screenwriter and author Anthony Horowitz, "Foyle's War" ran on ITV for thirteen years, opening in October 2002 and closing in January 2015. Set in England during and shortly after the Second World War, primarily in the town of Hastings, Sussex, the series follows Detective Chief Superintendent Christopher Foyle (Michael Kitchen), a widower known for his methodical approach, integrity, and pursuit of justice. Despite his desire to contribute directly to the war effort, his requests for transfer are denied, leading him to investigate crimes on the home front, including murder, profiteering, and corruption amid wartime chaos. He is assisted by his driver, Samantha "Sam" Stewart (Honeysuckle Weeks), and Detective Sergeant Paul Milner (Anthony Howell), a former soldier who lost a leg in combat. In later seasons, after retiring from the police force, Foyle is recruited by MI5 to tackle cases involving Cold War espionage. The series is acclaimed for its historical accuracy, complex character development, and intricate storylines that explore the moral complexities of wartime Britain.

**Стало:**

Two colleagues work the cases with him: Detective Sergeant Paul Milner (Anthony Howell), a former soldier who came home from combat without a leg, and Samantha "Sam" Stewart (Honeysuckle Weeks), his driver. The series is acclaimed for its historical accuracy, complex character development, and intricate storylines that explore the moral complexities of wartime Britain.

Helm travels to Copenhagen, where he teams up with Freya Carlson (Sharon Tate), a guide who turns out to be a secret British agent. *Ряд 18 → 9 слов, и эти 9 — само название рассказа в кавычках, оно оставлено дословно; сохранены Riggan Thomson, Michael Keaton, Birdman, Broadway, Raymond Carver, эго и былая слава. ⚠️ ЗАПИСЬ ОСТАЁТСЯ КОПИЕЙ: после правки в описании ещё 10 слов подряд («best picture best director best original screenplay and best…») — нужна вторая правка.*

**Было:**

> An American black comedy from 2014, directed by Alejandro González Iñárritu. The film follows Riggan Thomson (Michael Keaton), an actor famous for his role as the superhero Birdman, who strives to regain his popularity by staging a Broadway adaptation of Raymond Carver's short story "What We Talk About When We Talk About Love." Facing challenges in his personal and professional life, Riggan battles internal demons and the voice of Birdman, representing his ego and past fame. During the preparation for the premiere, he encounters difficulties, including replacing an actor with Mike Shiner (Edward Norton), a talented but challenging individual. His daughter Sam (Emma Stone), recently out of rehab, works as his assistant, and their relationship is strained. His ex-wife Sylvia (Amy Ryan) supports him despite past disagreements. The cast also includes Lesley (Naomi Watts), who dreams of Broadway success, and Laura (Andrea Riseborough), with whom Riggan has a romantic involvement. The film explores themes of self-identity, ego struggle, criticism, and the search for meaning in art. Filmed to appear as a single continuous shot, it creates a sense of uninterrupted action. The film received critical acclaim and won four Academy Awards, including Best Picture, Best Director, Best Original Screenplay, and Best Cinematography.

## `gonjiam-haunted-asylum-zsvnktnh`

*Ряд 18 → 3 слова: одно длинное предложение разбито на три, ведущим стал Ha-joon, состав группы вынесен отдельно; сохранены шесть человек, трое мужчин и трое женщин, все шесть имён, «Horror Times», Yoo Je-yoon, реальная больница Gonjiam.*

**Было:**

> A 2018 South Korean found footage horror film directed by Jung Bum-shik. Based on the real-life Gonjiam Psychiatric Hospital, the plot follows a group of six individuals: three women (Ah-yeon, Charlotte, and Ji-hyun) and three men (Sung-hoon, Seung-wook, and Je-yoon), who join the owner of the YouTube channel "Horror Times," Ha-joon (Yoo Je-yoon), to conduct a live broadcast from the abandoned Gonjiam Psychiatric Hospital, notorious for its dark history. Ha-joon remains at a base camp near the hospital, overseeing the broadcast, while the others enter the building. Unknown to the women and the viewers, Ha-joon, Sung-hoon, and Seung-wook have pre-scripted the scenario with fake scares to create an effect. However, as the exploration progresses, the group encounters inexplicable and terrifying phenomena beyond their staging. They discover a photograph of the hospital's patients and staff in the director's office, along with a doll that they notice moves on its own and matches one held by a patient in the photograph. Events become increasingly sinister: objects move by themselves, participants begin to disappear, and attempts to leave the building prove futile. Ultimately, all members fall victim to the hospital's supernatural forces, and their broadcast cuts off, leaving viewers puzzled about their fate. The film explores themes of fear, curiosity, and the consequences of meddling with the unknown.

**Стало:**

## `the-king-of-comedy-ehwbj2eq`

*Ряд 18 → 5 слов по всей записи; сохранены 1982, Мартин Скорсезе, Пол Д. Зиммерман (фамилия остаётся в хвосте предложения), жанр — перевёрнут строй: подлежащим стал режиссёр.*

**Было:**

> A 1982 American satirical black comedy film directed by Martin Scorsese and written by Paul D. Zimmerman. The story follows Rupert Pupkin (Robert De Niro), a delusional and aspiring stand-up comedian obsessed with fame. After a chance encounter with renowned talk show host Jerry Langford (Jerry Lewis), Rupert becomes convinced that his big break is imminent. However, his persistent attempts to secure a spot on Langford's show are met with rejection. Immersed in elaborate fantasies where he and Langford are close friends and colleagues, Pupkin's detachment from reality becomes evident. Desperate, Rupert teams up with fellow fan Masha (Sandra Bernhard) to kidnap Langford, demanding a guest spot on his show as ransom. With no alternative, the producers agree, and Pupkin delivers a stand-up routine blending humor with confessions about his life and crime. His performance is well-received, and after serving a prison sentence, Rupert emerges as a celebrity, publishing an autobiography and hosting his own television show. The film delves into themes of obsession, delusion, and the pursuit of fame, offering a critique of celebrity culture and media influence. Despite positive critical reviews, the film was a box office failure, grossing only $2.5 million against a $19 million budget. Over time, "The King of Comedy" has been recognized for its prescient portrayal of the entertainment industry and media dynamics.

**Стало:**

> A 2022 American action comedy film directed by Tom Gormican, who co-wrote the screenplay with Kevin Etten. The film follows Hollywood actor Nicolas Cage (played by himself), whose career is in decline. To improve his financial situation, he agrees to attend the birthday party of Spanish billionaire Javi Gutierrez (Pedro Pascal) in Mallorca for $1 million. Upon arrival, Cage learns from CIA agents Vivian (Tiffany Haddish) and Martin (Ike Barinholtz) that Javi is suspected of leading a criminal cartel and kidnapping the daughter of a Catalonian presidential candidate. Cage agrees to assist the CIA by pretending to be interested in a collaborative project with Javi to gather information. However, a genuine friendship develops between him and Javi, leading Cage to question Javi's guilt. The situation becomes more complicated when Cage's ex-wife Olivia (Sharon Horgan) and their daughter Addy (Lily Sheen) arrive on the island, invited by Javi to help Cage mend his family relationships. It is soon revealed that Javi's cousin, Lucas (Paco León), the true head of the cartel, is behind the kidnapping. Cage and Javi team up to rescue Addy and Maria (Katrin Vankova), the politician's daughter. Through a tense operation, they manage to free the hostages and eliminate the threat. The film concludes with the premiere of Cage's new movie based on these events and his reconciliation with his family.

> An American action-adventure video game from 2003, developed by Shiny Entertainment and published by Infogrames under the Atari brand. The game's storyline runs parallel to the events of the film "The Matrix Reloaded" and features over an hour of original footage written and directed by Lana and Lilly Wachowski, starring the film's cast. Players control two characters: Niobe (Jada Pinkett Smith), captain of the ship "Logos," and Ghost (Anthony Wong), her first mate and weapons specialist. The gameplay combines third-person shooting, hand-to-hand combat, and driving sequences, allowing the use of bullet time ("Focus") to perform acrobatic moves and dodge bullets. The game received mixed reviews from critics but was commercially successful, selling over 5 million copies by May 2004.

The story once again centers on the crested penguin Cody Maverick (voiced by Jeremy Shada), who, despite his acquired surfing experience, feels a stagnation in his career and craves new adventures. His friend, Chicken Joe (Jon Heder), now a recognized surfer, supports Cody in his aspirations. The legendary surfing team "The Hang 5" (voiced by WWE professional wrestlers: John Cena as J.C., The Undertaker as The Undertaker, Triple H as Hunter, Saraya Bevis as Paige, and Vince McMahon as Mr. McMahon), known for their extreme stunts and pursuit of the most dangerous waves, arrives on Pen-Gu Island. Inspired by their fame, Cody seeks to join their team and embark on a quest for the mythical "Big Wave," which, according to legend, is the most perilous and exhilarating challenge for any surfer. However, to prove his worthiness, Cody must undergo a series of trials, demonstrating not only his surfing prowess but also teamwork, courage, and dedication. Throughout the preparation, he confronts inner doubts, overcomes fears, and learns the importance of collaboration. Lani Aliikai (Melissa Sturm), Cody's friend and lifeguard, supports him throughout the journey, helping him realize the true values of friendship and selflessness. In the film's climax, the team reaches the location where, according to legend, the "Big Wave" appears. Facing the majestic and dangerous force of nature, Cody and his companions must utilize all their skills and unity to tackle the challenge and prove to themselves and others that true glory lies not in victories, but in overcoming personal limitations and supporting friends. The film combines humor, dynamic surfing scenes, and elements related to wrestling culture, providing viewers with an engaging and inspiring spectacle.

**Стало:**
